一座定居所，凡能看出并唤起本地意识——街坊的需要原是共同的需要——又能对满足这类需要的市政措施出一把大力，它便尽到了自己最宝贵的职能。拿我们头一回改良附近街面铺装的努力作个例：我们发现，好容易争得一条街上过半数业主同意换新路面，那位市议员却借着他替一位业主「把捐税压下去」的热心服务，把整套计划卡死了。那条街久久是一滩叫人吃惊的湿烂雪松木块，孩子们有时陷在里面——他们把幸存的一块木块放在很快灌满窟窿的水里漂着玩，别的木块早被抽走当柴烧了。可是等到我们能够证明：街面正是叫邻近一家工厂的重载货车碾成了雪松纸浆，重铺的费用理应出自公共基金，而不该压在穷业主身上——我们发现大家能同心主张改革摊派铺路捐的办法，连那位市议员本人也不得不加入这样一场众望所归的运动。在赫尔会所开了两个冬季会的第十九选区改良协会（Nineteenth Ward Improvement Association），是第一个能在本地铺路问题上真正咬动一口的公民团体。他们请了一位专家盯着铺路施工，确保他们那一半铺路款花得不冤。相信地价会因此看涨——这个共同的目标把附近较兴旺的人聚拢来，多少像赫尔会所合作煤会（Hull House Cooperative Coal Association）把穷人聚拢来一样。

===Original===

I remember that during the second campaign against our alderman, Governor Pingree of Michigan came to visit at Hull House. He said that the stronghold of such a man was not the place in which to start municipal regeneration; that good aldermen should be elected from the promising wards first, until a majority of honest men in the city council should make politics unprofitable for corrupt men. We replied that it was difficult to divide Chicago into good and bad wards, but that a new organization called the Municipal Voters’ League was attempting to give to the well-meaning voter in each ward throughout the city accurate information concerning the candidates and their relation, past and present, to vital issues. One of our trustees who was most active in inaugurating this League always said that his nineteenth-ward experience had convinced him of the unity of city politics, and that he constantly used our campaign as a challenge to the unaroused citizens living in wards less conspicuously corrupt.

Certainly the need for civic cooperation was obvious in many directions, and in none more strikingly than in that organized effort which must be carried on unceasingly if young people are to be protected from the darker and coarser dangers of the city. The cooperation between Hull House and the Juvenile Protective Association came about gradually, and it seems now almost inevitably. From our earliest days we saw many boys constantly arrested, and I had a number of most enlightening experiences in the police station with an Irish lad whose mother upon her deathbed had begged me “to look after him.” We were distressed by the gangs of very little boys who would sally forth with an enterprising leader in search of old brass and iron, sometimes breaking into empty houses for the sake of the faucets or lead pipe which they would sell for a good price to a junk dealer. With the money thus obtained they would buy cigarettes and beer or even candy, which could be conspicuously consumed in the alleys where they might enjoy the excitement of being seen and suspected by the “coppers.” From the third year of Hull House, one of the residents held a semiofficial position in the nearest police station; at least, the sergeant agreed to give her provisional charge of every boy and girl under arrest for a trivial offense.

===Chinese===

我记得，针对我们那位市议员的第二场竞选期间，密歇根州的平格里州长（Governor Pingree of Michigan）来赫尔会所做客。他说，这种人的堡垒，不是启动市政更新的地方；应当先从有希望的选区选出好市议员，直到市议会里诚实的人占了多数，叫搞腐败的人在政治里无利可图。我们回答，芝加哥难分好选区坏选区；不过一个叫市政选民同盟（Municipal Voters' League）的新组织，正设法给全城各选区怀有善意的选民提供准确的信息，介绍候选人及其同重大问题过去和现在的关系。我们的一位理事，是发起这个同盟最出力的人，他总说，第十九选区的经历使他确信城市政治是一体的；他常常拿我们的竞选，去挑动那些住在腐败不那么扎眼的选区、还未醒来的公民。

公民协作的需要，显然向着许多方向，而最扎眼的，莫过于那桩必须不停进行的事业——要保护年轻人不受城市更黑暗更粗粝的危险，非它不可。赫尔会所同少年保护协会（Juvenile Protective Association）的合作是逐步达成的，如今看来几乎是必然。从最早的岁月起，我们就看见许多男孩动辄被抓；在警察局里，我陪一个爱尔兰小伙子打过不少交道，大开过眼界——他母亲临终时求我「照看他」。我们为那些一伙一伙的小男孩苦恼：他们跟着一个有闯劲的头儿出发，去搜罗旧铜旧铁，有时为了水龙头和铅管溜进空屋，好卖给收破烂的，卖个好价钱。得来的钱买香烟和啤酒，甚至买糖，招摇地在巷子里享用，享受被「警察」看见、被疑心的刺激。从赫尔会所的第三年起，一位居民在最近的警察局里挂了个半官半私的职分；至少，警佐答应把每个因小过失被抓的男孩女孩，都先交她暂行看管。

===Original===

Mrs. Stevens, who performed this work for several years, became the first probation officer of the Juvenile Court when it was established in Cook County in 1899. She was the sole probation officer at first, but at the time of her death, which occurred at Hull House in 1900, she was the senior officer of a corps of six. Her entire experience had fitted her to deal wisely with wayward children. She had gone into a New England cotton mill at the age of thirteen, where she had promptly lost the index finger of her right hand, through “carelessness” she was told, and no one then seemed to understand that freedom from care was the prerogative of childhood. Later she became a typesetter and was one of the first women in America to become a member of the typographical union, retaining her card through all the later years of editorial work. As the Juvenile Court developed, the committee of public-spirited citizens who first supplied only Mrs. Stevens’ salary later maintained a corps of twenty-two such officers; several of these were Hull House residents who brought to the house for many years a sad little procession of children struggling against all sorts of handicaps. When legislation was secured which placed the probation officers upon the payroll of the county, it was a challenge to the efficiency of the civil service method of appointment to obtain by examination men and women fitted for this delicate human task. As one of five people asked by the civil service commission to conduct this first examination for probation officers, I became convinced that we were but at the beginning of the nonpolitical method of selecting public servants, but even stiff and unbending as the examination may be, it is still our hope of political salvation.

In 1907, the Juvenile Court was housed in a model court building of its own, containing a detention home and equipped with a competent staff. The committee of citizens largely responsible for this result thereupon turned their attention to the conditions which the records of the court indicated had led to the alarming amount of juvenile delinquency and crime. They organized the Juvenile Protective Association, whose twenty-two officers meet weekly at Hull House with their executive committee to report what they have found and to discuss city conditions affecting the lives of children and young people.

===Chinese===

办理这桩事务好几年的史蒂文斯太太（Mrs. Stevens），1899年库克县设立少年法庭（Juvenile Court）时，成了第一任缓刑监督官。起初她一人独任，到1900年她在赫尔会所去世时，已是六人队伍里的资深官员。她一生的经历，使她善于同歧路的孩子打交道。她十三岁进新英格兰的纱厂，很快便丢了右手食指——人家告诉她是「不小心」——那时还没有人懂得，无忧无虑本是童年的特权。后来她做了排字工，是美国最早加入印刷工会的妇女之一，此后多年做编辑工作，会籍一直保持着。少年法庭发展起来，最初只负担史蒂文斯太太一人薪俸的那班热心公益的公民，后来维持了一支二十二人的官员队伍；其中几位便是赫尔会所的居民，多年里带进会所一列伤感的孩子队伍，同各种各样的厄运挣扎。等到立法把缓刑监督官列上县里的薪册，考验便落到考绩任用的效力上：要靠考试甄选出适合这份微妙人情的差事的男人女人。我应文官委员会之邀，同另外四人一起主持这头一次缓刑监督官考试；我由此确信，非政治地遴选公仆，我们才刚起步；可是无论考试多么僵硬死板，它仍是我们政治得救的希望。

1907年，少年法庭搬进了自有的模范法庭大楼，附设拘留之家，配有胜任的职员。为这个结果出了大力的公民委员会，随即把注意力转向法庭记录所指明的境况——正是它们造成少年过失与犯罪的惊人数量。他们组织了少年保护协会；它的二十二名官员每星期在赫尔会所同执行委员会碰头，报告所见，讨论影响儿童与青少年生活的城市境况。

===Original===

The association discovers that there are certain temptations into which children so habitually fall that it is evident that the average child cannot withstand them. An overwhelming mass of data is accumulated showing the need of enforcing existing legislation and of securing new legislation, but it also indicates a hundred other directions in which the young people who so gaily walk our streets, often to their own destruction, need safeguarding and protection.

The effort of the association to treat the youth of the city with consideration and understanding has rallied the most unexpected forces to its standard. Quite as the basic needs of life are supplied solely by those who make money out of the business, so the modern city has assumed that the craving for pleasure must be ministered to only by the sordid. This assumption, however, in a large measure broke down as soon as the Juvenile Protective Association courageously put it to the test. After persistent prosecutions, but also after many friendly interviews, the Druggists’ Association itself prosecutes those of its members who sell indecent postal cards; the Saloon Keepers’ Protective Association not only declines to protect members who sell liquor to minors, but now takes drastic action to prevent such sales; the Retail Grocers’ Association forbids the selling of tobacco to minors; the Association of Department Store Managers not only increased the vigilance in their waiting rooms by supplying more matrons, but as a body they have become regular contributors to the association; the special watchmen in all the railroad yards agree not to arrest trespassing boys but to report them to the association; the firms manufacturing moving picture films not only submit their films to a volunteer inspection committee, but ask for suggestions in regard to new matter; and the Five-Cent Theaters arrange for “stunts” which shall deal with the subject of public health and morals, when the lecturers provided are entertaining as well as instructive.

===Chinese===

协会发现，有些诱惑孩子们惯常落进去，可见寻常的孩子抵不住。堆积如山的材料证明，现有立法需要执行，新的立法需要争取；可它还指着一百个别的方向——那些欢欢喜喜走在我们街上的年轻人，往往走向毁灭——那里需要防护与保护。

协会本着体谅与理解对待城中年轻人的努力，把最意想不到的力量召到了它的旗下。恰如生活的基本需要，向来只有从中赚钱的人才肯供给，现代城市也一向假定：对快乐的渴求，只配由污浊的行当去伺候。可是少年保护协会一鼓勇气把它拿去检验，这假定便大半垮了。经过坚持的控告，也经过许多次友好的商谈：药剂师公会自己起来控告发售下流明信片的会员了；酒馆主保护协会不但拒绝保护向未成年者卖酒的会员，现在还采取严厉措施防止这类买卖了；零售杂货商协会禁止把烟草卖给未成年者；百货商店经理协会不但增派女管理员、加强候见室的看护，而且作为一个团体，成了协会的经常捐款人；各铁路调车场的专职看守员同意不逮捕擅自闯入的孩子，而把他们报告给协会；制造电影胶片的公司不但把片子交给一个志愿审查委员会，还就新题材征求意见；五分钱戏院（Five-Cent Theaters）也安排起涉及公共卫生与道德的插演节目，只要提供的讲解人既有趣又有益。

===Original===

It is not difficult to arouse the impulse of protection for the young, which would doubtless dictate the daily acts of many a bartender and poolroom keeper if they could only indulge it without giving their rivals an advantage. When this difficulty is removed by an evenhanded enforcement of the law, that simple kindliness which the innocent always evoke goes from one to another like a slowly spreading flame of good will. Doubtless the most rewarding experience in any such undertaking as that of the Juvenile Protective Association is the warm and intelligent cooperation coming from unexpected sources﻿—official and commercial as well as philanthropic. Upon the suggestion of the association, social centers have been opened in various parts of the city, disused buildings turned into recreation rooms, vacant lots made into gardens, hiking parties organized for country excursions, bathing beaches established on the lake front, and public schools opened for social purposes. Through the efforts of public-spirited citizens a medical clinic and a Psychopathic Institute have become associated with the Juvenile Court of Chicago, in addition to which an exhaustive study of court-records has been completed. To this carefully collected data concerning the abnormal child, the Juvenile Protective Association hopes in time to add knowledge of the normal child who lives under the most adverse city conditions.

It was not without hope that I might be able to forward in the public school system the solution of some of these problems of delinquency so dependent upon truancy and ill-adapted education that I became a member of the Chicago Board of Education in July, 1905. It is impossible to write of the situation as it became dramatized in half a dozen strong personalities, but the entire experience was so illuminating as to the difficulties and limitations of democratic government that it would be unfair in a chapter on Civic Cooperation not to attempt an outline.

===Chinese===

唤起保护年轻人的冲动并不难；只要不必付出让对手占便宜的风险，这份冲动无疑会支配多少酒保、多少台球房老板的日常行为。这一层难处一由法律一视同仁的执行去掉，无辜者自然引起的那种单纯善意，便从一个人传给另一个人，像一团慢慢铺开的好意的火。无疑，在少年保护协会这样的事业里，最丰厚的报偿，是来自意想不到的方面的热烈而明白的合作——官方的、商业的，同慈善的一样。在协会的建议下，全城各处开设了社会活动中心，闲置的房屋改成了游艺室，空地辟成了园圃，组织了远足队到乡间旅行，湖滨设立了浴场，公立学校为社会用途开放了。经热心公益的公民们出力，一所医务所和一所心理病态研究所（Psychopathic Institute）同芝加哥少年法庭挂起钩来，此外还完成了一项对法庭记录的穷尽研究。关于异常儿童的这批细心收集的材料之外，少年保护协会希望假以时日，再添上关于在最不利城市境况下生活的正常儿童的知识。

正因为抱着也许能在公立学校制度里推动解决其中若干问题的希望——少年过失同逃学、同不适切的教育关系那样深——我于1905年7月进了芝加哥教育委员会（Chicago Board of Education）。局面如何凝聚在六七个强硬的人格里，变成了一出戏，这没法细写；可整段经历对民主政府的困难与局限的启发太大了，写公民协作的一章若不作个概述，便不公平。

===Original===

Even the briefest statement, however, necessitates a review of the preceding few years. For a decade the Chicago school teachers, or rather a majority of them who were organized into the Teachers’ Federation, had been engaged in a conflict with the Board of Education both for more adequate salaries and for more self-direction in the conduct of the schools. In pursuance of the first object, they had attacked the tax dodger along the entire line of his defense, from the curbstone to the Supreme Court. They began with an intricate investigation which uncovered the fact that in 1899, $235,000,000 of value of public utility corporations paid nothing in taxes. The Teachers’ Federation brought a suit which was prosecuted through the Supreme Court of Illinois and resulted in an order entered against the State Board of Equalization, demanding that it tax the corporations mentioned in the bill. In spite of the fact that the defendant companies sought federal aid and obtained an order which restrained the payment of a portion of the tax, each year since 1900, the Chicago Board of Education has benefited to the extent of more than a quarter of a million dollars. Although this result had been attained through the unaided efforts of the teachers, to their surprise and indignation their salaries were not increased. The Teachers’ Federation, therefore, brought a suit against the Board of Education for the advance which had been promised them three years earlier but never paid. The decision of the lower court was in their favor, but the Board of Education appealed the case, and this was the situation when the seven new members appointed by Mayor Dunne in 1905 took their seats. The conservative public suspected that these new members were merely representatives of the Teachers’ Federation. This opinion was founded upon the fact that Judge Dunne had rendered a favorable decision in the teachers’ suit and that the teachers had been very active in the campaign which had resulted in his election as mayor of the city. It seemed obvious that the teachers had entered into politics for the sake of securing their own representatives on the Board of Education. These suspicions were, of course, only confirmed when the new board voted to withdraw the suit of their predecessors from the Appellate Court and to act upon the decision of the lower court.

===Chinese===

然而哪怕最简短的陈述，也须回溯前面几年。十年来，芝加哥的教师们——或者说其中组成了教师联合会（Teachers' Federation）的多数——一直在同教育委员会争执，一为更像样的薪水，二为办学的更多自主。为着第一个目标，他们沿着逃税者防线的全长一路攻打，从街沿石一直打到最高法院。他们先做了一番缜密的调查，揭出这样一桩事实：1899年，公用事业公司有值两亿三千五百万元的财产分文未纳税。教师联合会提起诉讼，一路打到伊利诺伊州最高法院，弄到一道针对州均税委员会（State Board of Equalization）的命令，责成它对状书中列举的公司征税。尽管被告公司求助于联邦法院，弄到一道命令暂缓缴纳部分税款，但自1900年起，芝加哥教育委员会每年受益都超过二十五万元。这结果虽是教师们孤军挣来的，他们的薪水却没有加，这使他们又惊又愤。教师联合会于是为三年前许给他们而从未兑现的加薪起诉教育委员会。下级法院判他们胜诉，委员会上诉——市长邓恩（Mayor Dunne）1905年任命的七名新委员就职时，局面正是如此。保守的公众疑心这些新委员不过是教师联合会的代表。这看法有个依据：邓恩法官曾在教师的讼案里作过有利判决，而教师们在把他送上市长宝座的那场竞选中出过大力。教师们为了在教育委员会里安插自己的代表而涉足政治，这似乎是明摆着的。等新委员会表决撤回前任在上诉法院的诉讼、照下级法院判决执行，这些疑心自然又深了一层。

===Original===

The teachers, on the other hand, defended their long effort in the courts, the State Board of Equalization, and the Legislature against the charge of “dragging the schools into politics,” and declared that the exposure of the indifference and cupidity of the politicians was a well-deserved rebuke, and that it was the politicians who had brought the schools to the verge of financial ruin; they further insisted that the levy and collection of taxes, tenure of office, and pensions to civil servants in Chicago were all entangled with the traction situation, which in their minds at least had come to be an example of the struggle between the democratic and plutocratic administration of city affairs. The new appointees to the School Board represented no concerted policy of any kind, but were for the most part adherents to the new education. The teachers, confident that their cause was identical with the principles advocated by such educators as Colonel Parker, were therefore sure that the plans of the “new education” members would of necessity coincide with the plans of the Teachers’ Federation. In one sense the situation was an epitome of Mayor Dunne’s entire administration, which was founded upon the belief that if those citizens representing social ideals and reform principles were but appointed to office, public welfare must be established.

===Chinese===

教师们呢，反过来为自己在法院、在州均税委员会、在州议会里那番长期努力辩护，驳「把学校拖进政治」的指控；他们声明，揭出政客们的冷漠与贪心是一记应得的耳光，把学校拖到破产边缘的正是政客；他们还坚持说，芝加哥的征税与收税、官员任期、公务员年金，全都同电车问题纠缠在一起——至少在他们心里，那已经是城市事务民主管理与财阀管理之争的一个标本。教育委员会的新受任者并不代表什么一致的政纲，多半是新教育的信奉者。教师们自信他们的事业同帕克上校（Colonel Parker）一流教育家主张的原则本是一回事，于是认定「新教育」派委员的计划必然同教师联合会的计划吻合。从某种意义上说，这个局面正是邓恩市长整个施政的缩影——它奠在一个信念上：只要把代表社会理想与改革原则的公民任命到职位上，公共福利自然就立起来了。

===Original===

During my tenure of office I many times talked to the officers of the Teachers’ Federation, but I was seldom able to follow their suggestions and, although I gladly cooperated in their plans for a better pension system and other matters, only once did I try to influence the policy of the Federation. When the withheld salaries were finally paid to the representatives of the Federation who had brought suit and were divided among the members who had suffered both financially and professionally during this long legal struggle, I was most anxious that the division should voluntarily be extended to all of the teachers who had experienced a loss of salary although they were not members of the Federation. It seemed to me a striking opportunity to refute the charge that the Federation was self-seeking and to put the whole long effort in the minds of the public, exactly where it belonged, as one of devoted public service. But it was doubtless much easier for me to urge this altruistic policy than it was for those who had borne the heat and burden of the day to act upon it.

The second object of the Teachers’ Federation also entailed much stress and storm. At the time of the financial stringency, and largely as a result of it, the Board had made the first substantial advance in a teacher’s salary dependent upon a so-called promotional examination, half of which was upon academic subjects entailing a long and severe preparation. The teachers resented this upon two lines of argument: first, that the scheme was unprofessional in that the teacher was advanced on her capacity as a student rather than on her professional ability; and, second, that it added an intolerable and unnecessary burden to her already overfull day. The administration, on the other hand, contended with much justice that there was a constant danger in a great public school system that teachers lose pliancy and the open mind, and that many of them had obviously grown mechanical and indifferent. The conservative public approved the promotional examinations as the symbol of an advancing educational standard, and their sympathy with the superintendent was increased because they continually resented the affiliation of the Teachers’ Federation with the Chicago Federation of Labor, which had taken place several years before the election of Mayor Dunne on his traction platform.

===Chinese===

任职期间，我多次同教师联合会的干事们谈话，却难得能照他们的建议办；虽然我乐于在他们的养老金改良计划等事情上合作，想影响联合会政策的事，我只做过一回。当扣住的薪水终于付给提起诉讼的联合会的代表们、分发到这场漫长讼争里经济和职业都吃了亏的会员们手中时，我最热切希望的，是这份分派自愿扩大到所有受过减薪损失的教师——哪怕他们不是会员。在我看来，这正是驳斥「联合会有私心」的绝好机会，好把这桩长期努力在公众心里安放在它本来该在的位置——一件鞠躬尽瘁的公务。可是，叫那些担过全天暑热与重担的人照此办理，无疑比我在旁边劝这样的利他政策难得多。

教师联合会的第二个目标，也招来许多风波。金融吃紧的那些年，也多半因为金融吃紧，委员会头一回把教师薪水的实质性加薪，同一场所谓的晋级考试挂钩，考试一半考学理科目，须得长期严酷的备考。教师们沿两条理由抗议：其一，这办法外行，因为教师的晋级凭的是她当学生的本事，不是她做教师的本领；其二，这给她本已满得溢出的一天，又添了一副吃不消的多余负担。行政那面呢，也不无道理地坚持：一个庞大的公立学校制度里，教师失去柔韧、失去开明的心，是常有的危险，她们中有许多人分明已经变得机械、冷漠。保守的公众赞成晋级考试，把它看作教育水准上升的象征；他们又因为一贯愤慨教师联合会同芝加哥劳工联合会（Chicago Federation of Labor）的结盟——那是在邓恩市长挟着他的电车政纲当选之前几年——对总监的同情又添了几分。

===Original===

This much talked of affiliation between the teachers and the trades-unionists had been, at least in the first instance, but one more tactic in the long struggle against the tax-dodging corporations. The Teachers’ Federation had won in their first skirmish against that public indifference which is generated in the accumulation of wealth and which has for its nucleus successful commercial men. When they found themselves in need of further legislation to keep the offending corporations under control, they naturally turned for political influence and votes to the organization representing workingmen. The affiliation had none of the sinister meaning so often attached to it. The Teachers’ Federation never obtained a charter from the American Federation of Labor, and its main interest always centered in the legislative committee.

===Chinese===

这桩人人谈论的教师同工会员的结盟，至少当初不过是反对逃税公司那场长期斗争里的又一步棋。教师联合会对那种发家致富的岁月里养出来的公共冷漠——它的核心正是得志的商人们——赢下了第一场遭遇战。待到需要进一步立法来管住那些作恶的公司，他们自然转向代表工人的组织去借政治影响和选票。这结盟并没有常被人加上的那种不祥的意思。教师联合会从未向美国劳工联合会领过特许状，它的重心始终落在立法委员会上。

===Original===

And yet this statement of the difference between the majority of the grade-school teachers and the Chicago School Board is totally inadequate, for the difficulties were stubborn and lay far back in the long effort of public school administration in America to free itself from the rule and exploitation of politics. In every city for many years the politician had secured positions for his friends as teachers and janitors; he had received a rake-off in the contract for every new building or coal supply or adoption of schoolbooks. In the long struggle against this political corruption, the one remedy continually advocated was the transfer of authority in all educational matters from the Board to the superintendent. The one cure for “pull” and corruption was the authority of the “expert.” The rules and records of the Chicago Board of Education are full of relics of this long struggle honestly waged by honest men, who unfortunately became content with the ideals of an “efficient business administration.” These businessmen established an able superintendent with a large salary, with his tenure of office secured by State law so that he would not be disturbed by the wrath of the balked politician. They instituted impersonal examinations for the teachers both as to entrance into the system and promotion, and they proceeded “to hold the superintendent responsible” for smooth-running schools. All this, however, dangerously approximated the commercialistic ideal of high salaries only for the management with the final test of a small expense account and a large output.

===Chinese===

然而，单单摆出小学教师多数同芝加哥教育委员会之间的分歧，是远远不够的；这些难处顽固得很，根子扎在美国公立学校行政摆脱政治的统治与盘剥的多年努力里。多年来，每个城市里的政客都替朋友谋过教师和校工的位置；每盖一栋新楼、每订一批煤、每采用一批教科书，他都要抽一笔好处。在这场反对政治腐败的长期斗争里，人们不断倡导的一味药，是把一切教育事项的权柄从委员会移交给总监。「门路」与腐败的唯一解药，是「专家」的权威。芝加哥教育委员会的规程与记录里，满是这场诚实人打过的诚实斗争的遗物；不幸的是，这些诚实人渐渐满足于「有效率的企业管理」的理想。这些生意人设了一位能干的总监，给他高薪，用州的法律保住他的任期，叫被挡了财路的政客的怒火动不得他。他们为教师设了非人格的考试，进制度和晋级都要考；他们进而「叫总监负起责任」，学校须得运转顺滑。而这一切，都危险地逼近了那种商业主义的理想：只有管理层拿高薪，最终的标准是开支账小、出产大。

===Original===

In this long struggle for a quarter of a century to free the public schools from political interference, in Chicago at least, the high wall of defense erected around the school system in order “to keep the rascals out” unfortunately so restricted the teachers inside the system that they had no space in which to move about freely and the more adventurous of them fairly panted for light and air. Any attempt to lower the wall for the sake of the teachers within was regarded as giving an opportunity to the politicians without, and they were often openly accused, with a show of truth, of being in league with each other. Whenever the Dunne members of the Board attempted to secure more liberty for the teachers, we were warned by tales of former difficulties with the politicians, and it seemed impossible that the struggle so long the focus of attention should recede into the dullness of the achieved and allow the energy of the Board to be free for new effort.

The whole situation between the superintendent supported by a majority of the Board and the Teachers’ Federation had become an epitome of the struggle between efficiency and democracy; on one side a well-intentioned expression of the bureaucracy necessary in a large system but which under pressure had become unnecessarily self-assertive, and on the other side a fairly militant demand for self-government made in the name of freedom. Both sides inevitably exaggerated the difficulties of the situation, and both felt that they were standing by important principles.

I certainly played a most inglorious part in this unnecessary conflict; I was chairman of the School Management Committee during one year when a majority of the members seemed to me exasperatingly conservative, and during another year when they were frustratingly radical, and I was of course highly unsatisfactory to both. Certainly a plan to retain the undoubted benefit of required study for teachers in such wise as to lessen its burden, and various schemes devised to shift the emphasis from scholarship to professional work, were mostly impatiently repudiated by the Teachers’ Federation, and when one badly mutilated plan finally passed the Board, it was most reluctantly administered by the superintendent.

===Chinese===

在这场四分之一个世纪的把公立学校从政治干涉里解放出来的斗争里，至少在芝加哥，为了「把无赖关在外面」而围着学校制度筑起的那堵高墙，不幸也把制度里面的教师圈得没有转身的余地，里面更有些冒险气质的，简直喘不上气来。为了墙里的教师而把墙降低一点的任何尝试，都被看作给墙外的政客开方便之门；两边勾结的指控，也常带着几分貌似真实的证据公开落到他们头上。委员会里的邓恩派每想为教师多争一点自由，就有从前同政客斗法的老故事来警告我们；那场久为众目所注的斗争，似乎怎么也不肯退到「已成之功」的沉寂里去，好叫委员会的精力腾出来干新的事业。

总监背后有委员会的多数，对面是教师联合会——整个局面成了效率对民主之争的缩影：一边是一种善意的官僚制的表现——大制度少不了官僚制，可它在压力之下变得不必要的自作主张；另一边是打着自由旗号的、相当好斗的自治要求。两边都免不了把处境的难处夸大，又都自觉是在坚守重大的原则。

在这场不必要的冲突里，我当然扮演了一个极不光彩的角色。有一年，我做学校管理委员会的主席，委员会多数在我看来保守得叫人生气；又一年，他们激进得叫人丧气；我呢，自然两面都看我极不顺眼。诚然，有一个既保住教师必修研习的实益、又减轻其负担的方案，还有几个把重心从学问挪向专业功夫的办法，多半被教师联合会不耐烦地顶了回来；等一个被改得面目全非的方案终于过了委员会，总监执行起来又是百般勉强。

===Original===

I at least became convinced that partisans would never tolerate the use of stepping-stones. They are much too impatient to look on while their beloved scheme is unstably balanced, and they would rather see it tumble into the stream at once than to have it brought to dry land in any such halfhearted fashion. Before my School Board experience, I thought that life had taught me at least one hard-earned lesson, that existing arrangements and the hoped for improvements must be mediated and reconciled to each other, that the new must be dovetailed into the old as it were, if it were to endure; but on the School Board I discerned that all such efforts were looked upon as compromising and unworthy, by both partisans. In the general disorder and public excitement resulting from the illegal dismissal of a majority of the “Dunne” board and their reinstatement by a court decision, I found myself belonging to neither party. During the months following the upheaval and the loss of my most vigorous colleagues, under the regime of men representing the leading Commercial Club of the city who honestly believed that they were rescuing the schools from a condition of chaos, I saw one beloved measure after another withdrawn. Although the new president scrupulously gave me the floor in the defense of each, it was impossible to consider them upon their merits in the lurid light which at the moment enveloped all the plans of the “uplifters.” Thus the building of smaller schoolrooms, such as in New York mechanically avoid overcrowding, the extension of the truant rooms so successfully inaugurated, the multiplication of school playgrounds, and many another cherished plan was thrown out or at least indefinitely postponed.

===Chinese===

至少我由此确信：党徒们决不能容忍垫脚石的用法。眼看心爱的方案摇摇欲坠地立着，他们太没有耐心了；宁可它一头栽进河里，也不肯这样半心半意地把它送上岸。进教育委员会之前，我以为人生至少教过我一条用高价换来的道理：既有的安排同盼望的改良，必须彼此调和折中，新的须得榫进旧的，才立得久；可是在委员会里我看清，两边的党徒都把这类努力看作妥协、看作失格。多数「邓恩派」委员遭非法解职、又经法院判决复职，随之而来的一片混乱与公众激愤中，我发觉自己哪一党也不属于了。大变动之后那几个月里，我最得力的同事纷纷离去，城里首屈一指的商业俱乐部（Commercial Club）的人物当家——他们诚心相信是在把学校从混沌中抢救出来——我眼看一项心爱的措施接着一项被撤销。新会长每次都一丝不苟地让我发言为它们辩护，可是在那一刻罩住「鼓吹进步者」一切计划的那种血红的火光里，谁也没法就事论事地考量它们。于是，像纽约那样机械地避免拥挤的小教室的建筑、办得卓有成效的旷课生教室的扩充、学校操场的倍增，还有许多心爱的计划，或者被扔了出去，或者至少无限期搁置了。

===Original===

The final discrediting of Mayor Dunne’s appointees to the School Board affords a very interesting study in social psychology; the newspapers had so constantly reflected and intensified the ideals of a business Board, and had so persistently ridiculed various administration plans for the municipal ownership of street railways, that from the beginning any attempt the new Board made to discuss educational matters only excited their derision and contempt. Some of these discussions were lengthy and disorderly and deserved the discipline of ridicule, but others which were well conducted and in which educational problems were seriously set forth by men of authority were ridiculed quite as sharply. I recall the surprise and indignation of a University professor who had consented to speak at a meeting arranged in the Board rooms, when next morning his nonpartisan and careful disquisition had been twisted into the most arrant uplift nonsense and so connected with a fake newspaper report of a trial marriage address delivered, not by himself, but by a colleague, that a leading clergyman of the city, having read the newspaper account, felt impelled to preach a sermon, calling upon all decent people to rally against the doctrines which were being taught to the children by an immoral School Board. As the bewildered professor had lectured in response to my invitation, I endeavored to find the animus of the complication, but neither from editor in chief nor from the reporter could I discover anything more sinister than that the public expected a good story out of these School Board “talk fests,” and that any man who even momentarily allied himself with a radical administration must expect to be ridiculed by those papers which considered the traction policy of the administration both foolish and dangerous.

===Chinese===

邓恩市长任命的教育委员最后落得的坏名声，是社会心理学上一个很有趣的研究题目。报纸那样不断地映照并加剧「企业型委员会」的理想，那样坚持不懈地讥笑市政营电车之类的种种施政方案，以致新委员会从一开始想讨论教育问题，就只招来它们的讥诮与轻蔑。有些讨论冗长而混乱，也确实该受讥诮的纪律；可另一些办得妥帖、由权威人士郑重陈述教育问题的讨论，被讥笑得一样尖刻。我记得一位大学教授，应允到委员会会室安排的一次会上讲话，第二天早晨，他那不偏不倚、细针密缕的论述，被拧成了最露骨的「进步」呓语，还同一则假新闻挂在一起——报上说有人宣讲试婚——其实讲那话的不是他，是一位同事——城里一位领头的牧师读了报道，觉得非站出来讲道不可，号召一切正派人齐心反对这个不道德的教育委员会教给孩子们的教义。因为这位晕头转向的教授是应我的邀请来演讲的，我便设法去寻这场纠葛的祸心；可是不论总编辑还是记者，我探出的都不比这更多：公众指望从教育委员会的这些「闲谈会」上得一篇好故事；谁哪怕暂时同激进的一届政府沾了边，就得等着被那些认定政府的电车政策又蠢又险的报纸取笑。

===Original===

As I myself was treated with uniform courtesy by the leading papers, I may perhaps here record my discouragement over this complicated difficulty of open discussion, for democratic government is founded upon the assumption that differing policies shall be freely discussed and that each party shall have an opportunity for at least a partisan presentation of its contentions. This attitude of the newspapers was doubtless intensified because the Dunne School Board had instituted a lawsuit challenging the validity of the lease for the school ground occupied by a newspaper building. This suit has since been decided in favor of the newspaper, and it may be that in their resentment they felt justified in doing everything possible to minimize the prosecuting School Board. I am, however, inclined to think that the newspapers but reflected an opinion honestly held by many people, and that their constant and partisan presentation of this opinion clearly demonstrates one of the greatest difficulties of governmental administration in a city grown too large for verbal discussions of public affairs.

===Chinese===

既然几家大报待我一向客气，我或许可以在这里记下我对公开讨论这桩复杂难处的灰心：民主政府立在一个假定上——不同的政策须得自由讨论，每一方至少有机会作一回偏袒己方的陈述。报纸的这个态度无疑又添了一层火，因为邓恩教育委员会提起了一桩诉讼，质疑一家报馆大楼所占校址租约的效力。这案子后来判给了报馆；也许是在怨愤里，他们觉得把兴讼的教育委员会贬到最小，怎样做都问心无愧。不过我倾向于认为，报纸不过是反映了许许多多人诚心持有的意见；它们对这意见连篇累牍、偏执一方的陈述，清楚显出了城市治理的最大难处之一——城市已经大得口头讨论公共事务不再可能。

===Original===

It is difficult to close this chapter without a reference to the efforts made in Chicago to secure the municipal franchise for women. During two long periods of agitation for a new city charter, a representative body of women appealed to the public, to the charter convention, and to the Illinois legislature for this very reasonable provision. During the campaign when I acted as chairman of the federation of a hundred women’s organizations, nothing impressed me so forcibly as the fact that the response came from bodies of women representing the most varied traditions. We were joined by a church society of hundreds of Lutheran women, because Scandinavian women had exercised the municipal franchise since the seventeenth century and had found American cities strangely conservative; by organizations of working women who had keenly felt the need of the municipal franchise in order to secure for their workshops the most rudimentary sanitation and the consideration which the vote alone obtains for workingmen; by federations of mothers’ meetings, who were interested in clean milk and the extension of kindergartens; by property-owning women, who had been powerless to protest against unjust taxation; by organizations of professional women, of university students, and of collegiate alumnae; and by women’s clubs interested in municipal reforms. There was a complete absence of the traditional women’s rights clamor, but much impressive testimony from busy and useful women that they had reached the place where they needed the franchise in order to carry on their own affairs. A striking witness as to the need of the ballot, even for the women who are restricted to the most primitive and traditional activities, occurred when some Russian women waited upon me to ask whether under the new charter they could vote for covered markets and so get rid of the shocking Chicago grime upon all their food; and when some neighboring Italian women sent me word that they would certainly vote for public washhouses if they ever had the chance to vote at all. It was all so human, so spontaneous, and so direct that it really seemed as if the time must be ripe for political expression of that public concern on the part of women which had so long been forced to seek indirection.

===Chinese===

这一章很难不提一提芝加哥争取妇女市政选举权的努力。在为新市章奔走鼓动的两段长长的时期里，一个有代表性的妇女团体向公众、向市章大会、向伊利诺伊州议会吁求这一条完全合理的条款。我担任一百个妇女组织的联合会的主席的那场竞选中，最叫我不能忘的，是响应来自代表着最不相同传统的妇女团体。加入我们的有几百名信义会妇女的教会团体——因为斯堪的纳维亚妇女从十七世纪起就行使市政选举权，觉得美国的城市出奇地保守；有劳动妇女的组织——她们痛感需要市政选举权，好为她们作坊争到最起码的卫生和对工人的那点体恤——那只有选票才争得到；有母亲会联合会——她们关心洁净的牛奶和幼稚园的推广；有有产的妇女——她们对不公的捐税一直无从抗议；有职业妇女、大学生、女大学毕业生的组织；还有关心市政改良的妇女俱乐部。传统的女权叫嚷一点没有，倒有许多忙碌而有用的妇女动人的证言：她们走到了这一步，需要选举权来办自己的事。关于选票之需要，连限于最原始最传统活动的妇女也不例外——有个现成的见证：几位俄国妇女来找我，问新市章之下她们能不能投票选有棚的菜市场，好摆脱芝加哥那层吓人的、蒙在一切吃食上的污垢；邻近的几位意大利妇女也捎话来，说只要有一日真能投票，她们一定投给公共洗衣房。这一切那样合乎人情、那样自发、那样直接，真叫人觉得：妇女这一份久被逼着绕道表达的公共关切，其政治表达的时机想必已经熟了。

===Original===

None of these busy women wished to take the place of men nor to influence them in the direction of men’s affairs, but they did seek an opportunity to cooperate directly in civic life through the use of the ballot in regard to their own affairs.

A Municipal Museum which was established in the Chicago public library building several years ago, largely through the activity of a group of women who had served as jurors in the departments of social economy, of education, and of sanitation in the World’s Fair at St. Louis, showed nothing more clearly than that it is impossible to divide any of these departments from the political life of the modern city which is constantly forced to enlarge the boundary of its activity.

===Chinese===

这些忙碌的妇女没有一个想取代男人，也没有一个想照男人的事务的方向去影响男人；她们要的只是一个机会，凭选票就自己的事务直接参与公民生活。

几年前设在芝加哥公共图书馆馆舍里的市政博物馆（Municipal Museum），主要是靠一班妇女的努力建起来的——她们曾在圣路易斯（St. Louis）世界博览会上，出任社会经济、教育、卫生各科的评审——这座博物馆再清楚不过地表明：这几科中的任何一科，都无法同现代城市的政治生活割开——城市正被逼着不断扩大它活动的边界。

## XV / 十五
===Original===

The Value of Social Clubs

From the early days at Hull House, social clubs composed of English speaking American born young people grew apace. So eager were they for social life that no mistakes in management could drive them away. I remember one enthusiastic leader who read aloud to a club a translation of *Antigone*, which she had selected because she believed that the great themes of the Greek poets were best suited to young people. She came into the club room one evening in time to hear the president call the restive members to order with the statement, “You might just as well keep quiet for she is bound to finish it, and the quicker she gets to reading, the longer time we’ll have for dancing.” And yet the same club leader had the pleasure of lending four copies of the drama to four of the members, and one young man almost literally committed the entire play to memory.

===Chinese===

社交俱乐部的价值

从赫尔馆的早期起，由说英语的美国本土青年组成的社交俱乐部迅速发展。他们渴望社交生活，管理上出什么错也赶不走他们。我记得一位热心的领队给一个俱乐部朗读《安提戈涅》（*Antigone*）的译本，她选这个本子，是因为她相信希腊诗人的宏大主题最适于年轻人。一天晚上她走进俱乐部活动室，正好听见会长招呼躁动的会员们安静：「你们不如干脆别吵，反正她横竖要把它读完；她越早开读，咱们跳舞的时间就越长。」然而正是这位领队，后来有幸把那部戏剧的四个本子借给了四名会员，有一个年轻人几乎是逐字把整部戏背了下来。

===Original===

On the whole we were much impressed by the great desire for self-improvement, for study and debate, exhibited by many of the young men. This very tendency, in fact, brought one of the most promising of our earlier clubs to an untimely end. The young men in the club, twenty in number, had grown much irritated by the frivolity of the girls during their long debates, and had finally proposed that three of the most “frivolous” be expelled. Pending a final vote, the three culprits appealed to certain of their friends who were members of the Hull House Men’s Club, between whom and the debating young men the incident became the cause of a quarrel so bitter that at length it led to a shooting. Fortunately the shot missed fire, or it may have been true that it was “only intended for a scare,” but at any rate, we were all thoroughly frightened by this manifestation of the hot blood which the defense of woman has so often evoked. After many efforts to bring about a reconciliation, the debating club of twenty young men and the seventeen young women, who either were or pretended to be sober minded, rented a hall a mile west of Hull House severing their connection with us because their ambitious and right-minded efforts had been unappreciated, basing this on the ground that we had not urged the expulsion of the so-called “tough” members of the Men’s Club, who had been involved in the difficulty. The seceding club invited me to the first meeting in their new quarters that I might present to them my version of the situation and set forth the incident from the standpoint of Hull House. The discussion I had with the young people that evening has always remained with me as one of the moments of illumination which life in a Settlement so often affords. In response to my position that a desire to avoid all that was “tough” meant to walk only in the paths of smug self-seeking and personal improvement leading straight into the pit of self-righteousness and petty achievement and was exactly what the Settlement did not stand for, they contended with much justice that ambitious young people were obliged for their own reputation, if not for their own morals, to avoid all connection with that which bordered on the tough, and that it was quite another matter for the Hull House residents who could afford a more generous judgment.

===Chinese===

总体上，许多年轻人表现出的对自我提高、对学习和辩论的巨大渴望，给了我们很深的印象。正是这种倾向，事实上使我们早期最有前途的俱乐部之一过早收场。俱乐部里二十个年轻男子，被姑娘们在他们冗长辩论期间的轻浮弄得大为光火，最后提议把三个最「轻浮」的开除出去。表决未定，三位被告向她们在赫尔馆男子俱乐部里的几个朋友求助；在她们与辩论的年轻男子之间，这件事成了一场争吵的由头，吵得那么凶，最后弄到开枪。幸而那枪哑了火，或者也许真像说的「只想吓唬吓唬」；但无论如何，这种护卫女性常常激发出的热血的显现，把我们全吓坏了。经过多次调和的努力，那个二十人的青年辩论俱乐部和十七位或是端庄或装作端庄的姑娘，在赫尔馆以西一英里租了个会场，同我们断了关系，理由是她们那些志向远大、动机端正的努力不受赏识，根据是我们没有敦促开除卷入此事的男子俱乐部里那些所谓的「粗坯」会员。分裂出去的俱乐部邀我出席他们新会场的第一次集会，好让我陈述我对局面的说法，从赫尔馆的立场讲述这件事。那天晚上我同这些年轻人的讨论，一直留在我心里，算作定居所生活常常赐下的豁然开朗的时刻之一。我说，一心要躲开一切「粗坯」，就意味着只走在沾沾自喜的自私自利和个人小算盘的路上，直通自以为义和琐碎成就的坑——这恰恰是定居所不主张的；他们则针锋相对，而且颇有点道理：有志向的年轻人，即使不为自己的品行计，也为自己的名声计，不得不回避一切近乎「粗坯」的东西；赫尔馆的居民们负担得起更宽厚的判断，那完全是另一回事。

===Original===

It was in vain I urged that life teaches us nothing more inevitably than that right and wrong are most confusingly confounded; that the blackest wrong may be within our own motives, and that at the best, right will not dazzle us by its radiant shining and can only be found by exerting patience and discrimination. They still maintained their wholesome bourgeois position, which I am now quite ready to admit was most reasonable.

Of course there were many disappointments connected with these clubs when the rewards of political and commercial life easily drew the members away from the principles advocated in club meetings. One of the young men who had been a shining light in the advocacy of municipal reform deserted in the middle of a reform campaign because he had been offered a lucrative office in the city hall; another even after a course of lectures on business morality, “worked” the club itself to secure orders for custom-made clothing from samples of cloth he displayed, although the orders were filled by ready-made suits slightly refitted and delivered at double their original price. But nevertheless, there was much to cheer us as we gradually became acquainted with the daily living of the vigorous young men and women who filled to overflowing all the social clubs.

===Chinese===

我徒劳地申说：生活教给我们的，没有比这更必然的了——对与错混淆得最教人糊涂；最黑的错可能就在我们自己的动机里；而且说到底，对也绝不会以它的光芒四射晃花我们的眼，它只能靠耐心和分辨去找到。他们仍坚持他们那种健全的小市民立场——这一点我如今完全愿意承认是极有道理的。

当然，这些俱乐部也有许多失望，政治和商业生活的报酬轻易把会员们从俱乐部会议上鼓吹的原则身边拉走了。一个曾在鼓吹市政改革中熠熠发光的年轻人，在一次改革运动的半途倒戈了，因为市政厅给了他一个肥缺；另一个在上过一门商业道德课程之后，竟然「做」起俱乐部本身的生意来——展示布样为订制服装揽订单，尽管交付的是略微改了改的现成西装，价钱翻了一倍。但尽管如此，随着我们渐渐熟悉那些把社交俱乐部挤得满满当当的健壮青年男女的日常起居，还是有许多叫人振奋的东西。

===Original===

We have been much impressed during our twenty years, by the ready adaptation of city young people to the prosperity arising from their own increased wages or from the commercial success of their families. This quick adaptability is the great gift of the city child, his one reward for the hurried changing life which he has always led. The working girl has a distinct advantage in the task of transforming her whole family into the ways and connections of the prosperous when she works down town and becomes conversant with the manners and conditions of a cosmopolitan community. Therefore having lived in a Settlement twenty years, I see scores of young people who have successfully established themselves in life, and in my travels in the city and outside, I am constantly cheered by greetings from the rising young lawyer, the scholarly rabbi, the successful teacher, the prosperous young matron buying clothes for blooming children. “Don’t you remember me? I used to belong to a Hull House club.” I once asked one of these young people, a man who held a good position on a Chicago daily, what special thing Hull House had meant to him, and he promptly replied, “It was the first house I had ever been in where books and magazines just lay around as if there were plenty of them in the world. Don’t you remember how much I used to read at that little round table at the back of the library? To have people regard reading as a reasonable occupation changed the whole aspect of life to me and I began to have confidence in what I could do.”

===Chinese===

二十年间，城市年轻人对自己工资增长或家人生意成功带来的宽裕，那种随遇而安的适应，给了我们很深的印象。这种快速的适应力是城市孩子的大天赋，是他为一直过的匆忙多变的生活得到的唯一报酬。在市中心做工、谙熟了一个世界主义社会的风度和境况的职业少女，在把全家改造进兴旺者的做派和圈子这项任务上，占有明确的优势。因此在定居所住了二十年，我见到几十个成功立身的年轻人；在城里城外的旅途中，我不断受到招呼声的鼓舞——冉冉上升的年轻律师、学者气的拉比、成功的教师、为盛开的孩子们挑衣裳的殷实的年轻主妇。「您不记得我了吗？我从前是赫尔馆一个俱乐部的。」我曾问过其中一个——他在芝加哥一家日报有个好职位——赫尔馆对他有什么特别的意义；他立刻答道：「那是我进过的第一座屋子，书和杂志就那么随便搁着，好像世界上有的是。您不记得我从前在图书室后面那张小圆桌边读了多少书吗？有人把读书看作一件正经事，这改变了生活在我眼里的整个面貌，我开始对自己的能力有了信心。」

===Original===

Among the young men of the social clubs a large proportion of the Jewish ones at least obtain the advantages of a higher education. The parents make every sacrifice to help them through the high school after which the young men attend universities and professional schools, largely through their own efforts. From time to time they come back to us with their honors thick upon them; I remember one who returned with the prize in oratory from a contest between several western State universities, proudly testifying that he had obtained his confidence in our Henry Clay Club; another came back with a degree from Harvard University saying that he had made up his mind to go there the summer I read Royce’s “Aspects of Modern Philosophy” with a group of young men who had challenged my scathing remark that Herbert Spencer was not the only man who had ventured a solution of the riddles of the universe. Occasionally one of these learned young folk does not like to be reminded he once lived in our vicinity, but that happens rarely, and for the most part they are loyal to us in much the same spirit as they are to their own families and traditions. Sometimes they go further and tell us that the standards of tastes and code of manners which Hull House has enabled them to form, have made a very great difference in their perceptions and estimates of the larger world as well as in their own reception there. Five out of one club of twenty-five young men who had held together for eleven years, entered the University of Chicago but although the rest of the Club called them the “intellectuals,” the old friendships still held.

===Chinese===

社交俱乐部的年轻人里，犹太青年至少有很大一部分受了高等教育。父母作一切牺牲供他们读完中学，此后年轻人上大学和专业学校，大体靠自己的努力。他们时时带着一身荣光回到我们这里；我记得一个带着几所西部州立大学演讲比赛的头奖回来，自豪地作证说，他的信心是在我们的亨利·克莱俱乐部（Henry Clay Club）里得来的；另一个带着哈佛大学的学位回来，说正是我同一群年轻人读罗伊斯《现代哲学诸方面》的那个夏天，他打定主意要去那里——那群年轻人曾经挑战过我那句刻薄话：斯宾塞并不是唯一胆敢给宇宙之谜提出解答的人。偶尔这些有学问的年轻人不愿被人提起曾住在我们这一带，但那样的事很少；大体上，他们忠于我们，差不多像忠于自己的家族和传统一样。有时他们更进一步，告诉我们赫尔馆使他们形成的趣味标准和礼法规矩，对他们对大世界的感知和估量、对他们自己在那边受到的接纳，都起了很大的作用。一个相聚了十一年的二十五人俱乐部，有五人进了芝加哥大学；尽管其余会员管他们叫「知识分子」，旧的友谊还牢着呢。

===Original===

In addition to these rising young people given to debate and dramatics, and to the members of the public school alumni associations which meet in our rooms, there are hundreds of others who for years have come to Hull House frankly in search of that pleasure and recreation which all young things crave and which those who have spent long hours in a factory or shop demand as a right. For these young people all sorts of pleasure clubs have been cherished, and large dancing classes have been organized. One supreme gayety has come to be an annual event of such importance that it is talked of from year to year. For six weeks before St. Patrick’s day, a small group of residents put their best powers of invention and construction into preparation for a cotillion which is like a pageant in its gayety and vigor. The parents sit in the gallery, and the mothers appreciate more than anyone else perhaps, the value of this ball to which an invitation is so highly prized; although their standards of manners may differ widely from the conventional, they know full well when the companionship of the young people is safe and unsullied.

As an illustration of this difference in standard, I may instance an early Hull House picnic arranged by a club of young people, who found at the last moment that the club director could not go and accepted the offer of the mother of one of the club members to take charge of them. When they trooped back in the evening, tired and happy, they displayed a photograph of the group wherein each man’s arm was carefully placed about a girl; no feminine waist lacked an arm save that of the proud chaperon, who sat in the middle smiling upon all. Seeing that the photograph somewhat surprised us, the chaperon stoutly explained, “This may look queer to you, but there wasn’t one thing about that picnic that wasn’t nice,” and her statement was a perfectly truthful one.

===Chinese===

除了这些耽于辩论和戏剧的上升的年轻人，除了在我们房间里聚会的各公立学校校友会会员，还有几百个人，多年来坦率地为快乐和消遣来赫尔馆——一切年轻之物都渴求的快乐和消遣，凡在工厂或店铺里熬过长时数的人，把它当作一种权利来要求。为这些年轻人，滋养了各种各样的游乐俱乐部，组织了大型舞蹈班。有一场至高的狂欢成了每年一件大事，年年被人谈论。圣帕特里克节前六个星期，一小队居民把发明的本事和制作的本事全用上，筹备一场科蒂荣舞会——它的欢腾和劲头像一场庆典。父母们坐在楼座上；做母亲的比谁都领会这场舞会的价值——请柬是那样被人珍视；虽然她们的礼数标准可以同俗例相差很远，她们完全知道什么时候年轻人的交往是安全而清白的。

作为这种标准差异的一例，我可以举赫尔馆早年一次野餐，是一个青年俱乐部组织的；最后一刻发现俱乐部指导人来不了，便接受了一位会员母亲的提议，由她来照管他们。傍晚他们一群人又累又快活地回来，展示了一张合影，相片里每个男人的手臂都规规矩矩搂着一个姑娘；没有一条女人的腰缺了手臂，除了那位骄傲的陪护人，她坐在正中，朝大家微笑。见这照片让我们有点吃惊，陪护人振振有词地解释：「这在你们看来也许古怪，可那次野餐没有一处不体面。」她的话句句属实。

===Original===

Although more conventional customs are carefully enforced at our many parties and festivities, and while the dancing classes are as highly prized for the opportunity they afford for enforcing standards as for their ostensible aim, the residents at Hull House, in their efforts to provide opportunities for clean recreation, receive the most valued help from the experienced wisdom of the older women of the neighborhood. Bowen Hall is constantly used for dancing parties with soft drinks established in its foyer. The parties given by the Hull House clubs are by invitation and the young people themselves carefully maintain their standard of entrance so that the most cautious mother may feel safe when her daughter goes to one of our parties. No club festivity is permitted without the presence of a director; no young man under the influence of liquor is allowed; certain types of dancing often innocently started are strictly prohibited; and above all, early closing is insisted upon. This standardizing of pleasure has always seemed an obligation to the residents of Hull House, but we are, I hope, saved from that priggishness which young people so heartily resent, by the Mardi Gras dance and other festivities which the residents themselves arrange and successfully carry out.

In spite of our belief that the standards of a ball may be almost as valuable to those without as to those within, the residents are constantly concerned for those many young people in the neighborhood who are too hedonistic to submit to the discipline of a dancing class or even to the claim of a pleasure club, but who go about in freebooter fashion to find pleasure wherever it may be cheaply on sale.

===Chinese===

虽然在我们的许多聚会和庆典上，较俗例的规矩被小心执行；虽然舞蹈班之为人所重，一半在它们提供了贯彻标准的机缘，一如其表面上的目的；赫尔馆的居民们在提供清洁娱乐的努力中，最宝贵的帮助还是来自街区年长妇女们经验里的智慧。鲍恩厅（Bowen Hall）经常用作舞会，前厅设着软饮料。赫尔馆各俱乐部的聚会凭请柬入场，年轻人自己小心维护着入场的标准，最谨慎的母亲也可以放心让女儿来参加我们的聚会。没有指导人在场，不得举行任何俱乐部欢庆；不许醉酒的青年入场；某些常常无意间跳起来的舞步被严格禁止；而首要的是，坚持早散场。这种给快乐定标准，赫尔馆的居民一向看作一种义务；但我希望，居民们自己操办并成功举办的狂欢节（Mardi Gras）舞会和其他庆典，把我们从年轻人深恶痛绝的道学气里救了出来。

尽管我们相信，一场舞会的标准，对场外的人几乎同对场内的人一样有价值，居民们还是常常挂念街区里那些太享乐主义的年轻人：他们受不了舞蹈班的纪律，甚至受不了游乐俱乐部的要求，四处打着游击，哪里有便宜出卖的快乐就到哪里去找。

===Original===

Such young people, well meaning but impatient of control, become the easy victims of the worst type of public dance halls, and of even darker places, whose purposes are hidden under music and dancing. We were thoroughly frightened when we learned that during the year which ended last December, more than twenty-five thousand young people under the age of twenty-five passed through the Juvenile and Municipal Courts of Chicago﻿—approximately one out of every eighty of the entire population, or one out of every fifty-two of those under twenty-five years of age. One’s heart aches for these young people caught by the outside glitter of city gayety, who make such a feverish attempt to snatch it for themselves. The young people in our clubs are comparatively safe, but many instances come to the knowledge of Hull House residents which make us long for the time when the city, through more small parks, municipal gymnasiums, and schoolrooms open for recreation, can guard from disaster these young people who walk so carelessly on the edge of the pit.

The heedless girls believe that if they lived in big houses and possessed pianos and jewelry, the coveted social life would come to them. I know a Bohemian girl who surreptitiously saved her overtime wages until she had enough money to hire for a week a room with a piano in it where young men might come to call, as they could not do in her crowded untidy home. Of course she had no way of knowing the sort of young men who quickly discover an unprotected girl.

===Chinese===

这样的年轻人，心地不坏却受不了管束，成了最坏的公共舞厅、甚至更黑暗去处——其目的藏在音乐和跳舞底下——的轻易的牺牲品。当我们得知，到去年十二月为止的一年里，两万五千多名二十五岁以下的年轻人从芝加哥的少年法庭和市法院通过——大约占全人口的八十分之一，或二十五岁以下人口的五十二分之一——我们吓坏了。人为这些被城市欢华的外表光彩逮住的年轻人痛心，他们那样狂热地想为自己抓一把。我们俱乐部里的年轻人比较安全；但有许多事例传到赫尔馆居民的耳朵里，叫我们盼望城市早点靠更多的小公园、市立体操馆和开放作娱乐之用的教室，看住这些在深渊边上走得那么漫不经心的年轻人。

不顾一切的姑娘们相信，只要住上大房子、有了钢琴和首饰，渴望的社交生活自会找上门来。我认识一个波希米亚姑娘，偷偷攒下加班费，攒到够为一个星期租一间带钢琴的屋子，好让小伙子们来拜访——在她那拥挤邋遢的家里这是办不到的。当然，她没法知道什么样的年轻人会最快发现一个无人保护的姑娘。

===Original===

Another girl of American parentage who had come to Chicago to seek her fortune, found at the end of a year that sorting shipping receipts in a dark corner of a warehouse not only failed to accumulate riches but did not even bring the “attentions” which her quiet country home afforded. By dint of long sacrifice she had saved fifteen dollars; with five she bought an imitation sapphire necklace, and the balance she changed into a ten dollar bill. The evening her pathetic little snare was set, she walked home with one of the clerks in the establishment, told him that she had come into a fortune, and was obliged to wear the heirloom necklace to insure its safety, permitted him to see that she carried ten dollars in her glove for carfare, and conducted him to a handsome Prairie Avenue residence. There she gayly bade him goodbye and ran up the steps shutting herself in the vestibule from which she did not emerge until the dazzled and bewildered young man had vanished down the street.

Then there is the ever-recurring difficulty about dress; the insistence of the young to be gayly bedecked to the utter consternation of the hardworking parents who are paying for a house and lot. The Polish girl who stole five dollars from her employer’s till with which to buy a white dress for a church picnic was turned away from home by her indignant father who replaced the money to save the family honor, but would harbor no “thief” in a household of growing children who, in spite of the sister’s revolt, continued to be dressed in dark heavy clothes through all the hot summer. There are a multitude of working girls who for hours carry hair ribbons and jewelry in their pockets or stockings, for they can wear them only during the journey to and from work. Sometimes this desire to taste pleasure, to escape into a world of congenial companionship takes more elaborate forms and often ends disastrously. I recall a charming young girl, the oldest daughter of a respectable German family, whom I first saw one spring afternoon issuing from a tall factory. She wore a blue print gown which so deepened the blue of her eyes that Wordsworth’s line fairly sung itself:

“The pliant harebell swinging in the breeze

On some gray rock.”

===Chinese===

另一个美国血统的姑娘，来芝加哥闯天下，一年下来发现，在仓库一个黑暗角落里分拣货运单据不但攒不起钱来，连她寂静的乡下家里那份「殷勤」也捞不着。靠长久的省吃俭用她攒下十五块；五块买了一条仿制蓝宝石项链，余下的换成一张十块的钞票。那晚她那可怜的小圈套布好之后，她同店里的一个职员一道走回家，告诉他她得了一笔遗产，只好把传家的项链戴在身上保险；让他看见她手套里揣着十块钱作车费；把他领到草原大道（Prairie Avenue）一座漂亮的宅子前。到了那儿她快活地道了晚安，跑上台阶，把自己关进门厅，直到那看得眼花缭乱、晕头转向的年轻人消失在街尾，她才出来。

还有那个永远绕不开的衣裳问题：年轻人非要穿得花枝招展，把辛辛苦苦供房供地的父母惊得目瞪口呆。那个从雇主的钱柜里偷了五块钱、给教堂野餐买白裙子的波兰姑娘，被愤怒的父亲赶出了家门；父亲垫上了钱保全家里名声，可是再不肯在家里养一个「贼」——那一家的孩子还小，不管姐姐怎么反抗，整个炎热的长夏，他们照旧穿着深色厚重的衣裳。有许多职业少女，把发带和首饰一连几个钟头揣在口袋或长袜里，因为只有上下班的路上才穿得着。有时这种尝一口快乐、逃进一个投契伴侣世界的欲望，采取更繁复的形式，而且常常以灾难收场。我记得一个可爱的年轻姑娘，一户体面德国人家的长女，我第一次见到她，是一个春天的午后，她从一座高大的厂房里走出来。她穿着一件蓝印花布的裙子，把眼睛的蓝衬得那样深，华兹华斯（Wordsworth）的诗句简直自己唱了起来：

「柔顺的蓝铃花在微风里摇曳／在某处灰岩上。」

===Original===

I was grimly reminded of that moment a year later when I heard the tale of this seventeen-year-old girl, who had worked steadily in the same factory for four years before she resolved “to see life.” In order not to arouse her parents’ suspicions, she borrowed thirty dollars from one of those loan sharks who require no security from a pretty girl, so that she might start from home every morning as if to go to work. For three weeks she spent the first part of each dearly bought day in a department store where she lunched and unfortunately made some dubious acquaintances; in the afternoon she established herself in a theater and sat contentedly hour after hour watching the endless vaudeville until the usual time for returning home. At the end of each week she gave her parents her usual wage, but when her thirty dollars was exhausted it seemed unendurable that she should return to the monotony of the factory. In the light of her newly acquired experience she had learned that possibility which the city ever holds open to the restless girl.

===Chinese===

一年以后，当我听到这个十七岁姑娘的事，我冷冷地想起了那个时刻。她在同一家工厂稳稳当当做了四年，然后决心「见见世面」。为了不引起父母的疑心，她向那种不向漂亮姑娘要抵押的高利贷子借了三十块钱，好每天早晨照常从家里出发，像去上工的样子。有三个星期，她把每个高价买来的日子的头一段花在一家百货公司——在那儿吃午饭，不幸结交了几个可疑的相识；下午她安顿在一家戏院里，心满意足地一小时一小时坐着，看那没完没了的杂耍，直到惯常回家的钟点。每个周末她把惯常的工资交给父母；可三十块钱花完了，要她回到工厂的单调里去，似乎无法忍受了。凭她新得的经验，她已经知道了那个城市永远为不安分的姑娘敞开着的可能。

===Original===

That more such girls do not come to grief is due to those mothers who understand the insatiable demand for a good time, and if all of the mothers did understand, those pathetic statistics which show that four fifths of all prostitutes are under twenty years of age would be marvelously changed. We are told that “the will to live” is aroused in each baby by his mother’s irresistible desire to play with him, the physiological value of joy that a child is born, and that the high death rate in institutions is increased by “the discontented babies” whom no one persuades into living. Something of the same sort is necessary in that second birth at adolescence. The young people need affection and understanding each one for himself, if they are to be induced to live in an inheritance of decorum and safety and to understand the foundations upon which this orderly world rests. No one comprehends their needs so sympathetically as those mothers who iron the flimsy starched finery of their grownup daughters late into the night, and who pay for a red velvet parlor set on the installment plan, although the younger children may sadly need new shoes. These mothers apparently understand the sharp demand for social pleasure and do their best to respond to it, although at the same time they constantly minister to all the physical needs of an exigent family of little children. We often come to a realization of the truth of Walt Whitman’s statement, that one of the surest sources of wisdom is the mother of a large family.

===Chinese===

这样的姑娘没有更多地遭殃，要归功于那些懂得享乐之欲无法匾足的母亲们；倘若所有的母亲都懂，那些表明五分之四的妓女不满二十岁的可怜统计数字，会奇迹般地改观。有人告诉我们，「活下去的意志」是每个婴儿被母亲同他玩要的不可抗拒的欲望唤醒的——那是一个孩子诞生的喜悦的生理价值；也告诉我们，收容机构里所以死亡率高，是因为「不满的婴儿」没有人劝他们活。青春期的第二次诞生，也需要某种同样的东西。年轻人需要各人独得的感情和理解，才能被引导着活在体面与安全的遗产里，才能懂得这个有秩序的世界立在什么根基上。没有人比那些深夜里熨烫成年女儿们轻薄的浆硬盛装的母亲们更富同情地懂得；没有人比她们更懂得分期付款买下一堂红丝绒客厅家具，哪怕更小的孩子们苦等着新鞋。这些母亲显然懂得对社交快乐的尖锐要求，并且尽力应答它，尽管同时又不断伺候着一大家亟需照拂的小儿女。我们常常领悟到惠特曼那句话的真实：智慧最可靠的源头之一，是一个大家庭的母亲。

===Original===

It is but natural, perhaps, that the members of the Hull House Woman’s Club whose prosperity has given them some leisure and a chance to remove their own families to neighborhoods less full of temptations, should have offered their assistance in our attempt to provide recreation for these restless young people. In many instances their experience in the club itself has enabled them to perceive these needs. One day a Juvenile Court officer told me that a woman’s club member, who has a large family of her own and one boy sufficiently difficult, had undertaken to care for a ward of the Juvenile Court who lived only a block from her house, and that she had kept him in the path of rectitude for six months. In reply to my congratulations upon this successful bit of reform to the club woman herself, she said that she was quite ashamed that she had not undertaken the task earlier for she had for years known the boy’s mother who scrubbed a downtown office building, leaving home every evening at five and returning at eleven during the very time the boy could most easily find opportunities for wrongdoing. She said that her obligation toward this boy had not occurred to her until one day when the club members were making pillowcases for the Detention Home of the Juvenile Court, it suddenly seemed perfectly obvious that her share in the salvation of wayward children was to care for this particular boy and she had asked the Juvenile Court officer to commit him to her. She invited the boy to her house to supper every day that she might know just where he was at the crucial moment of twilight, and she adroitly managed to keep him under her own roof for the evening if she did not approve of the plans he had made. She concluded with the remark that it was queer that the sight of the boy himself hadn’t appealed to her, but that the suggestion had come to her in such a roundabout way.

===Chinese===

也许很自然，赫尔馆妇女俱乐部的会员们——家境宽裕给了她们一点闲暇，也有机会把自家搬进诱惑少些的街区——在我们为这些不安分的年轻人提供娱乐的努力中，主动伸出了援手。许多时候，俱乐部本身的经历使她们看得见这些需要。一天，一位少年法庭官员告诉我，俱乐部的一位会员——她自己儿女成群，还有一个男孩相当难管——主动照管起少年法庭的一个被监护的孩子，那孩子离她家只隔一个街区；她让他在正道上走了六个月。我向这位俱乐部妇女本人道贺这桩成功的小小改良，她答说，她很惭愧没有早点担当起来，因为她认识那孩子的母亲多年了；那位母亲在市中心一座办公楼里擦洗，每晚五点出门十一点回来，恰是那孩子最容易找到做坏事机会的时候。她说，直到有一天，俱乐部会员们正为少年法庭的拘留所做枕套，她忽然觉得再明白不过：她在拯救失足孩子这件事上的份，就是照管这个孩子；于是她请少年法庭官员把他交给她。她天天请那孩子来家里吃晚饭，好确切知道黄昏这个要紧关头他在哪儿；她若不赞成他打的算盘，就巧妙地把他留在家中过晚上。她收尾说，奇怪的是那孩子本人的样子从没打动过她，这主意竟是这么绕着弯儿来的。

===Original===

She was, of course, reflecting upon a common trait in human nature﻿—that we much more easily see the duty at hand when we see it in relation to the social duty of which it is a part. When she knew that an effort was being made throughout all the large cities in the United States to reclaim the wayward boy, to provide him with reasonable amusement, to give him his chance for growth and development, and when she became ready to take her share in that movement, she suddenly saw the concrete case which she had not recognized before.

We are slowly learning that social advance depends quite as much upon an increase in moral sensibility as it does upon a sense of duty, and of this one could cite many illustrations. I was at one time chairman of the Child Labor Committee in the General Federation of Woman’s Clubs, which sent out a schedule asking each club in the United States to report as nearly as possible all the working children under fourteen living in its vicinity. A Florida club filled out the schedule with an astonishing number of Cuban children who were at work in sugar mills, and the club members registered a complaint that our committee had sent the schedule too late, for if they had realized the conditions earlier, they might have presented a bill to the legislature which had now adjourned. Of course the children had been working in the sugar mills for years, and had probably gone back and forth under the very eyes of the club women, but the women had never seen them, much less felt any obligation to protect them, until they joined a club, and the club joined a Federation, and the Federation appointed a Child Labor Committee who sent them a schedule. With their quickened perceptions they then saw the rescue of these familiar children in the light of a social obligation. Through some such experiences the members of the Hull House Woman’s Club have obtained the power of seeing the concrete through the general and have entered into various undertakings.

===Chinese===

她当然是在反思人性中一个常见的特质：当手边的责任作为一项社会责任的一部分被看见时，我们容易得多地看见它。当她知道，全美国的大城市都在作出努力挽救失足的孩子，给他合理的娱乐，给他成长发展的机会；当她准备好在这场运动里尽自己的一份，她忽然看见了那个她从前不曾认出的具体案例。

我们正在慢慢懂得：社会的进步，靠道德敏感的增长，一点不亚于靠义务感；这一点可以举出许多例证。我一度担任妇女俱乐部总联合会（General Federation of Woman's Clubs）童工委员会的主席，联合会发出一份调查表，请美国每个俱乐部尽可能报告住在附近的所有十四岁以下的做工孩子。佛罗里达一家俱乐部填回了惊人的数字：一批在糖厂做工的古巴孩子；俱乐部会员们还提了意见，怪我们委员会把表寄得太迟了，因为倘若早一点意识到这些状况，她们本可以向已经休会的州议会提一项法案。当然，那些孩子在糖厂做了多年工，多半就在俱乐部妇女们的眼皮底下来来去去；可是她们从来没看见过他们，更谈不上觉得有什么保护他们的责任，直到她们加入了一个俱乐部，俱乐部加入了联合会，联合会设立了一个给她们寄调查表的童工委员会。感知一经敏锐，她们便在一种社会义务的光里看见了拯救这些熟悉的孩子。通过这样一些经历，赫尔馆妇女俱乐部的会员们获得了透过一般看见具体的能力，着手办起了各种各样的事业。

===Original===

Very early in its history the club formed what was called “A Social Extension Committee.” Once a month this committee gives parties to people in the neighborhood who for any reason seem forlorn and without much social pleasure. One evening they invited only Italian women, thereby crossing a distinct social “gulf,” for there certainly exists as great a sense of social difference between the prosperous Irish-American women and the South-Italian peasants as between any two sets of people in the city of Chicago. The Italian women, who were almost eastern in their habits, all stayed at home and sent their husbands, and the social extension committee entered the drawing room to find it occupied by rows of Italian workingmen, who seemed to prefer to sit in chairs along the wall. They were quite ready to be “socially extended,” but plainly puzzled as to what it was all about. The evening finally developed into a very successful party, not so much because the committee were equal to it, as because the Italian men rose to the occasion.

===Chinese===

俱乐部的历史很早便成立了一个所谓「社会拓展委员会」。每月一次，这个委员会给街区里那些因这样那样缘故显得孤苦、缺少社交乐趣的人们办聚会。一天晚上，她们只请了意大利妇女，由此跨越了一道分明的社会「鸿沟」；因为在兴旺的爱尔兰裔美国妇女同南意大利农民之间，那种社会差等的感觉，确乎同芝加哥城里任何两群人之间一样深。习惯近乎东方的意大利妇女们全都待在家里，派了丈夫前来；社会拓展委员会走进客厅，发现里面坐满一排排意大利工人，他们似乎宁愿沿墙坐成一圈椅子。他们满心乐意被「社会拓展」一下，可是对这一切究竟为着什么，显然一头雾水。那晚最后办成了一场非常成功的聚会，倒不那么多因为委员会应付得了，而是因为意大利男人们撑起了场面。

===Original===

Untiring pairs of them danced the tarantella; they sang Neapolitan songs; one of them performed some of those wonderful sleight-of-hand tricks so often seen on the streets of Naples; they explained the coral finger of St. Januarius which they wore; they politely ate the strange American refreshments; and when the evening was over, one of the committee said to me, “Do you know I am ashamed of the way I have always talked about dagoes, they are quite like other people, only one must take a little more pains with them. I have been nagging my husband to move off M Street because they are moving in, but I am going to try staying awhile and see if I can make a real acquaintance with some of them.” To my mind at that moment the speaker had passed from the region of the uncultivated person into the possibilities of the cultivated person. The former is bounded by a narrow outlook on life, unable to overcome differences of dress and habit, and his interests are slowly contracting within a circumscribed area; while the latter constantly tends to be more a citizen of the world because of his growing understanding of all kinds of people with their varying experiences. We send our young people to Europe that they may lose their provincialism and be able to judge their fellows by a more universal test, as we send them to college that they may attain the cultural background and a larger outlook; all of these it is possible to acquire in other ways, as this member of the woman’s club had discovered for herself.

===Chinese===

一对对不知疲倦的他们跳起塔兰泰拉；他们唱那不勒斯歌谣；其中一个表演了那不勒斯街头常见的那种绝妙的戏法；他们讲解自己佩戴的圣雅纳略（St. Januarius）珊瑚指；他们客气地吃下陌生的美国茶点；晚会结束时，委员会的一位会员对我说：「您知道吗，我一向那样讲意大利佬，我很惭愧；他们同别人完全一样，只是得多费一点心。我一直撺掇丈夫搬离M街，因为他们搬进来了；可我打算再住一阵子，看看能不能同他们中的一些人结成真正的相识。」在我当时看来，说话的人已经从不曾修养的人的地界，跨进了有修养的人的可能。前者被狭窄的人生眼界框住，克服不了衣著和习惯的差异，兴趣在一个划定的圈子里慢慢收缩；后者则因对形形色色的人及其各异经历的理解日渐增长，不断趋向于更是一个世界公民。我们把年轻人送去欧洲，好让他们丢掉乡土气、能用更普遍的标准评判同伴；正如送他们上大学，好让他们取得文化的底子和更大的眼界；这一切都可以用别的途径取得，正如这位妇女俱乐部会员自己发现的那样。

===Original===

This social extension committee under the leadership of an ex-president of the Club, a Hull House resident with a wide acquaintance, also discover many of those lonely people of which every city contains so large a number. We are only slowly apprehending the very real danger to the individual who fails to establish some sort of genuine relation with the people who surround him. We are all more or less familiar with the results of isolation in rural districts; the Brontë sisters have portrayed the hideous immorality and savagery of the remote dwellers on the bleak moorlands of northern England; Miss Wilkins has written of the overdeveloped will of the solitary New Englander; but tales still wait to be told of the isolated city dweller. In addition to the lonely young man recently come to town, and the country family who have not yet made their connections, are many other people who, because of temperament or from an estimate of themselves which will not permit them to make friends with the “people around here,” or who, because they are victims to a combination of circumstances, lead a life as lonely and untouched by the city about them as if they were in remote country districts. The very fact that it requires an effort to preserve isolation from the tenement-house life which flows all about them, makes the character stiffer and harsher than mere country solitude could do.

===Chinese===

这个社会拓展委员会，在俱乐部一位前会长、一位交游广阔的赫尔馆居民带领下，还发现许多孤独的人——每座城市里这种人数量都很庞大。我们只是慢慢才领会到：一个人若不能同四周的人建立起某种真实的关系，对他是非常实在的危险。我们对乡村地区隔绝的结果都比较熟悉了：勃朗特姐妹描绘了英格兰北部荒凉沼地上偏远住户骇人的不道德与蛮野；威尔金斯小姐写尽了孤独的新英格兰人过度发达的意志；可是城市里孤独者的故事，还有待讲述。除了初来城里的孤独青年、尚未接上关系的农家，还有许多别的人：或因性情，或因一种不许自己同「这一带的人」交朋友的自我估量，或因为境遇的凑合做了牺牲品，过着同四周的城市全不相涉的孤独生活，仿佛身处偏远的乡野。要保持住这种隔绝、不受四下流淌的出租屋生活浸染，本身就得费力气——这一事实，使性格比单纯的乡间孤寂所能造成的更僵硬、更粗粿。

===Original===

Many instances of this come into my mind; the faded, ladylike hairdresser, who came and went to her work for twenty years, carefully concealing her dwelling place from the “other people in the shop,” moving whenever they seemed too curious about it, and priding herself that no neighbor had ever “stepped inside her door,” and yet when discovered through an asthma which forced her to crave friendly offices, she was most responsive and even gay in a social atmosphere. Another woman made a long effort to conceal the poverty resulting from her husband’s inveterate gambling and to secure for her children the educational advantages to which her family had always been accustomed. Her five children, who are now university graduates, do not realize how hard and solitary was her early married life when we first knew her, and she was beginning to regret the isolation in which her children were being reared, for she saw that their lack of early companionship would always cripple their power to make friends. She was glad to avail herself of the social resources of Hull House for them, and at last even for herself.

The leader of the social extension committee has also been able, through her connection with the vacant lot garden movement in Chicago, to maintain a most flourishing “friendly club” largely composed of people who cultivate these garden plots. During the club evening at least, they regain something of the ease of the man who is being estimated by the bushels per acre of potatoes he has raised, and not by that flimsy city judgment so often based upon store clothes. Their jollity and enthusiasm are unbounded, expressing itself in clog dances and rousing old songs often in sharp contrast to the overworked, worn aspects of the members.

Of course there are surprising possibilities discovered through other clubs, in one of Greek women or in the “*circolo Italiano*,” for a social club often affords a sheltered space in which the gentler social usages may be exercised, as the more vigorous clubs afford a point of departure into larger social concerns.

===Chinese===

许多这样的事例浮上我心头：那位褪了色的、淑女派头的理发师，二十年来来去去地上工，小心向「店里的其他人」隐瞒住处，他们一显得好奇她就搬家，自诩从没有邻居「跨进过她的门」；可是当一场气喘逼得她渴求友好的照料、被人发现之后，她在社交的气氛里最有回应，甚至快活。另一个女人作了长期努力，隐瞒丈夫嗜赌造成的贫穷，为孩子们保全她的娘家一向习惯的教育机会。她的五个孩子如今都是大学毕业生，并不知道我们初识她时她早年的婚姻生活多么艰苦孤单；她也开始懊悔孩子们在被隔绝的状态中长大，因为她看出，幼年缺了伴侣，会永远削弱他们结交朋友的能力。她乐于为孩子们利用赫尔馆的社交资源，最后连为自己也利用了。

社会拓展委员会的会长，还凭着她在芝加哥空地园圃运动中的关系，维持着一个兴旺非常的「友谊俱乐部」，成员大半是耕种那些园圃的人。至少在俱乐部的晚上，他们找回了一点那样的自在——一个人按他每英亩收了几蒲式耳土豆来估量，而不是按那常常以成衣为据的轻飘飘的城市评判。他们的快活和热忱没有边际，木屐舞和响亮的老歌里流露出来，同会员们操劳过度、憔悴的面容形成鲜明的对照。

当然，别的俱乐部里也有惊人的可能被发现，在一个希腊妇女俱乐部，或在「*circolo Italiano*」（意大利人俱乐部）里；因为社交俱乐部常常提供一方有遮蔽的空间，温柔的社交仪节可以在里面演习，正如更有劲的俱乐部提供跳进更广大社会关切的出发点。

===Original===

The experiences of the Hull House Woman’s Club constantly react upon the family life of the members. Their husbands come with them to the annual midwinter reception, to club concerts and entertainments; the little children come to the May party, with its dancing and games; the older children, to the day in June when prizes are given to those sons and daughters of the members who present a good school record as graduates either from the eighth grade or from a high school.

It seemed, therefore, but a fit recognition of their efforts when the president of the club erected a building planned especially for their needs, with their own library and a hall large enough for their various social undertakings, although of course Bowen Hall is constantly put to many other uses.

It was under the leadership of this same able president that the club achieved its wider purposes and took its place with the other forces for city betterment. The club had begun, as nearly all women’s clubs do, upon the basis of self-improvement, although the foundations for this later development had been laid by one of their earliest presidents, who was the first probation officer of the Juvenile Court, and who had so shared her experiences with the club that each member felt the truth as well as the pathos of the lines inscribed on her memorial tablet erected in their club library:﻿—

“As more exposed to suffering and distress

Thence also more alive to tenderness.”

===Chinese===

赫尔馆妇女俱乐部的经历，不断反作用于会员们的家庭生活。丈夫们随她们来赴一年一度的仲冬招待会，来赴俱乐部的音乐会和游艺会；小孩子们来赴五月的游园会，有跳舞有游戏；大孩子们来赴六月的那一天——那一天给会员们那些以优异成绩从八年级或中学毕业的儿女发奖。

因此，当俱乐部会长竖起了一座专为她们的需要而设计的楼房——有她们自己的图书室和一个够大的厅供种种社交事业之用（虽则鲍恩厅当然还有许多别的用途不断用着）——这看来正是对她们努力的一个相称的认可。

正是在这位同样干练的会长的领导下，俱乐部实现了它更广的目标，在城市改善的各项力量中占了一席之地。这个俱乐部同几乎所有妇女俱乐部一样，是从自我提高起家的；不过这后来发展的根子，是她们最早的一位会长奠下的——她是少年法庭的第一位缓刑官，又那样同俱乐部分享过她的经历，以致每一位会员都感受到刻在她们俱乐部图书室里那块纪念牌上的诗句的真实与哀婉：

「既更多暴露于苦难与忧伤／便也更多活络于温柔。」

===Original===

Each woman had discovered opportunities in her own experience for this same tender understanding, and under its succeeding president, Mrs. Pelham, in its determination to be of use to the needy and distressed, the club developed many philanthropic undertakings from the humble beginnings of a linen chest kept constantly filled with clothing for the sick and poor. It required, however, an adequate knowledge of adverse city conditions so productive of juvenile delinquency and a sympathy which could enkindle itself in many others of diverse faiths and training, to arouse the club to its finest public spirit. This was done by a later president, Mrs. Bowen, who, as head of the Juvenile Protective Association, had learned that the moralized energy of a group is best fitted to cope with the complicated problems of a city; but it required ability of an unusual order to evoke a sense of social obligation from the very knowledge of adverse city conditions which the club members possessed, and to connect it with the many civic and philanthropic organizations of the city in such wise as to make it socially useful. This financial and representative connection with outside organizations, is valuable to the club only as it expresses its sympathy and kindliness at the same time in concrete form. A group of members who lunch with Mrs. Bowen each week at Hull House discuss, not only topics of public interest, sometimes with experts whom they have long known through their mutual undertakings, but also their own club affairs in the light of this larger knowledge.

Thus the value of social clubs broadens out in one’s mind to an instrument of companionship through which many may be led from a sense of isolation to one of civic responsibility, even as another type of club provides recreational facilities for those who have had only meaningless excitements, or, as a third type, opens new and interesting vistas of life to those who are ambitious.

===Chinese===

每个女人都在自己的经历里发现了这种温柔理解的机会；在下一任会长佩勒姆夫人（Mrs. Pelham）任内，俱乐部本着救助穷苦危难者的决心，从一只不断补充着病人和穷人衣物的贮衣箱这样卑微的起点，发展出许多慈善事业。然而，要把俱乐部唤起它最美好的公共精神，需要的是对滋生少年犯罪的恶劣城市状况的充分认识，和一种能在信仰训练各异的许多别人心里点燃自己的同情。这是后来的一位会长鲍恩夫人（Mrs. Bowen）做到的；她作为少年保护协会的负责人，早已懂得：一群人经过道德化的能量，最宜于对付一座城市的复杂问题；可是要从会员们本有的对恶劣城市状况的认识里唤出一种社会义务感，再把它同城里许多市政和慈善组织连接起来、使之在社会上有用，需要的是非常之才。这种同外界组织在财务和代表面上的联系，只有当它同时以具体的形式表达出俱乐部的同情与善意时，才对俱乐部有价值。每星期在赫尔馆同鲍恩夫人共进午餐的一群会员，不仅讨论公共关心的话题——有时同她们通过共同的事业早已熟识的专家们——还借着这更大的认识讨论俱乐部自己的事务。

社交俱乐部的价值于是在人心里扩展开来，成了一件结伴的工具：许多人可以借着它，从孤独感走到公民责任；正如另一类俱乐部为那些只受过无意义的刺激的人提供娱乐的设施；又如第三类，向有志向的人敞开崭新而有意思的人生远景。

===Original===

The entire organization of the social life at Hull House, while it has been fostered and directed by residents and others, has been largely pushed and vitalized from within by the club members themselves. Sir Walter Besant once told me that Hull House stood in his mind more nearly for the ideal of the “Palace of Delight” than did the “London People’s Palace” because we had depended upon the social resources of the people using it. He begged me not to allow Hull House to become too educational. He believed it much easier to develop a polytechnic institute than a large recreational center, but he doubted whether the former was as useful.

===Chinese===

赫尔馆社交生活的整个组织，虽然由居民和其他人培育指导，却在很大程度上由俱乐部会员们自己从内部推动、赋予生命。沃尔特·贝桑特爵士（Sir Walter Besant）曾对我说，在他心里，赫尔馆比「伦敦人民宫」更接近「欢乐宫」的理想，因为我们依靠的是使用它的人们的社交资源。他求我别让赫尔馆变得太教育化。他相信，办一所工艺学校要比办一个大型娱乐中心容易得多；可他怀疑前者是否同样有用。

===Original===

The social clubs form a basis of acquaintanceship for many people living in other parts of the city. Through friendly relations with individuals, which is perhaps the sanest method of approach, they are thus brought into contact, many of them for the first time, with the industrial and social problems challenging the moral resources of our contemporary life. During our twenty years hundreds of these nonresidents have directed clubs and classes, and have increased the number of Chicago citizens who are conversant with adverse social conditions and conscious that only by the unceasing devotion of each, according to his strength, shall the compulsions and hardships, the stupidities and cruelties of life be overcome. The number of people thus informed is constantly increasing in all our American cities, and they may in time remove the reproach of social neglect and indifference which has so long rested upon the citizens of the new world. I recall the experience of an Englishman who, not only because he was a member of the Queen’s Cabinet and bore a title, but also because he was an able statesman, was entertained with great enthusiasm by the leading citizens of Chicago. At a large dinner party he asked the lady sitting next to him what our tenement-house legislation was in regard to the cubic feet of air required for each occupant of a tenement bedroom; upon her disclaiming any knowledge of the subject, the inquiry was put to all the diners at the long table, all of whom showed surprise that they should be expected to possess this information. In telling me the incident afterward, the English guest said that such indifference could not have been found among the leading citizens of London, whose public spirit had been aroused to provide such housing conditions as should protect tenement dwellers at least from wanton loss of vitality and lowered industrial efficiency. When I met the same Englishman in London five years afterward, he immediately asked me whether Chicago citizens were still so indifferent to the conditions of the poor that they took no interest in their proper housing. I was quick with that defense which an American is obliged to use so often in Europe, that our very democracy so long presupposed that each citizen could care for himself that we are slow to develop a sense of social obligation.

===Chinese===

社交俱乐部为住在城里别处许多人打下了相识的基础。凭着同个人的友好关系——这也许是接近问题最健全的方法——他们由此接触到向当代生活的道德资源挑战的工业和社会问题，其中许多人是头一回。二十年间，几百位这样的非居民指导过俱乐部和班级，壮大了芝加哥市民的队伍——他们熟悉不利的社会状况，并且意识到：只有每个人各按其力不停地奉献，生活的强制与艰辛、愚蠢与残酷才会被克服。这样知情的人，在我们所有的美国城市里不断增多；假以时日，他们也许能除去久已落在新世界公民头上的那个疏忽冷漠的恶名。我记得一位英国人的经历：他不仅因为做过女王的内阁成员、带着头衔，也因为他是个能干的国务活动家，受到芝加哥头面市民极热情的款待。在一次大型宴会上，他问邻座的女士，我们关于出租屋卧室每个居住者所需立方英尺空气的立法是什么；她推说不知，这问题便被端给了长桌上的每一位客人，人人都露出惊讶，仿佛不该指望他们掌握这种信息。事后这位英国客人对我说起这件事时说，这种冷漠在伦敦的头面市民中间是找不到的，伦敦的公共精神早已被唤起，去提供那样的居住条件，至少保护出租屋的居民免遭生命力的无谓损耗和工业效率的降低。五年后我在伦敦又遇到这位英国人，他立刻问我，芝加哥的市民是否还是那样对穷人的境况漠不关心，连他们像样的住房都不放在心上。我赶紧搬出美国人在欧洲不得不常常使用的那套辩护：我们的民主太长时间预设每个公民都能照顾自己，所以我们发展社会义务感很慢。

===Original===

He smiled at the familiar phrases and was still inclined to attribute our indifference to sheer ignorance of social conditions.

The entire social development of Hull House is so unlike what I predicted twenty years ago, that I venture to quote from that ancient writing as an end to this chapter.

“The social organism has broken down through large districts of our great cities. Many of the people living there are very poor, the majority of them without leisure or energy for anything but the gain of subsistence.

“They live for the moment side by side, many of them without knowledge of each other, without fellowship, without local tradition or public spirit, without social organization of any kind.
