The first hearing before a United States Commissioner gave a verdict favorable to the Russian Government although this was afterward reversed by the Department of State in Washington. Partly to educate American sentiment, partly to express sympathy with the Russian refugees in their dire need, a series of public meetings was arranged in which the operations of the extradition treaty were discussed by many of us who had spoken at a meeting held in protest against its ratification fifteen years before. It is impossible for anyone unacquainted with the Russian colony to realize the consternation produced by this attempted extradition. I acted as treasurer of the fund collected to defray the expenses of halls and printing in the campaign against the policy of extradition and had many opportunities to talk with members of the colony. One old man, tearing his hair and beard as he spoke, declared that all his sons and grandsons might thus be sent back to Russia; in fact, all of the younger men in the colony might be extradited, for every high-spirited young Russian was, in a sense, a revolutionist.

Would it not provoke to ironic laughter that very nemesis which presides over the destinies of nations, if the most autocratic government yet remaining in civilization should succeed in utilizing for its own autocratic methods the youngest and most daring experiment in democratic government which the world has ever seen? Stranger results have followed a course of stupidity and injustice resulting from blindness and panic!

It is certainly true that if the decision of the federal office in Chicago had not been reversed by the department of state in Washington, the United States government would have been committed to return thousands of spirited young refugees to the punishments of the Russian autocracy.

===Chinese===

许多俄国人相信，阿弗布赫事件将被作为前奏，引向不断使用引渡条约来威摄国内外的革命者；1908年引渡一个住在芝加哥、名叫鲁多维茨的俄国革命者的企图，给了这信念某种印证。美国一位联邦专员主持的首次听证作出了有利于俄国政府的裁决，虽然后来被华盛顿国务院推翻了。一部分为了教育美国舆情，一部分为了向身处绝境的俄国难民表达同情，安排了一系列公开集会；在会上讨论引渡条约的运作的，有许多人正是十五年前在一次抗议批准该条约的集会上讲过话的。不熟悉俄国侨民地的人，无法想象这次引渡企图造成的惊惶。我担任了反对引渡政策运动中为会场和印刷募捐的基金司库，有许多机会同侨民地的成员交谈。一个老人一边说一边扯着自己的头发和胡子，宣称他的儿子孙子就这样都可能被遣返俄国；其实侨民地里所有的年轻人都可以被引渡，因为每一个血气万刚的俄国青年，在某种意义上都是革命者。

倘若文明中残存的最专制的政府，竟能利用世界所见最年轻最大胆的民主政府实验，来为它自己的专制方法服务，那岂不要惹得主持各民族命运的那位报应女神发出讥笑？可是盲目标和恐慌所生的愚蠢和不义，造成过更高奇得多的结果！

当然，如果芝加哥联邦机构的裁决没有被华盛顿国务院推翻，美国政府就得承诺把成千上万血气方刚的年轻难民送回俄国专制的惩罚之下去。

===Original===

It was perhaps significant of our need of what Napoleon called a “revival of civic morals” that the public appeal against such a reversal of our traditions had to be based largely upon the contributions to American progress made from other revolutions; the Puritans from the English, Lafayette from the French, Carl Schurz and many another able man from the German upheavals in the middle of the century.

A distinguished German scholar writing at the end of his long life a description of his friends of 1848 who made a gallant although premature effort to unite the German states and to secure a constitutional government, thus concludes: “But not a few saw the whole of their lives wrecked, either in prison or poverty, though they had done no wrong, and in many cases were the finest characters it has been my good fortune to know. They were before their time; the fruit was not ripe, as it was in 1871, and Germany but lost her best sons in those miserable years.” When the time is ripe in Russia, when she finally yields to those great forces which are molding and renovating contemporary life, when her Cavour and her Bismark finally throw into the first governmental forms all that yearning for juster human relations which the idealistic Russian revolutionists embody, we may look back upon these “miserable years” with a sense of chagrin at our lack of sympathy and understanding.

===Chinese===

反对这样一次对我们传统的反转，公众的呼吁竟不得不大部分建立在别的革命对美国进步的贡献上——英国革命之于清教徒，法国革命之于拉法耶特，本世纪中叶德国动乱之于卡尔·舒尔茨和许多另一个能干的人——这也许正标志着我们需要拿攼仑所说的那种「公民道德的复兴」。

一位杰出的德国学者在漫长一生的末尾，描写他1848年的朋友们——他们作了一次英勇然而早熟的尝试，想统一德意志各邦、争取一个立宪政府——如此作结：「但不少人眼看着整个一生毁了，或在狱中，或在贫困里，虽然他们没有做过错事，而且许多正是我有幸认识的最优秀的品格。他们走在了时代前面；果子还没有熟——像1871年那样熟——德国在那几个悲惨的年头里失去的，只是她最好的儿子。」当俄国的时机成熟，当她终于向那股塑造和更新当代生活的伟大力量让步，当她的加富尔和她的俾斯麦终于把理想主义的俄国革命者所体现的那种对更公正人际关系的渴望，全部投进最初的政府形式里去，那时我们回望这些「悲惨的年头」，会为我们自己的缺乏同情和理解感到懊恼。

===Original===

Again it is far from easy to comprehend the great Russian struggle. I recall a visit from the famous revolutionist Gershuni, who had escaped from Siberia in a barrel of cabbage rolled under the very fortress of the commandant himself, had made his way through Manchuria and China to San Francisco, and on his way back to Russia had stopped in Chicago for a few days. Three months later we heard of his death, and whenever I recall the conversation held with him, I find it invested with that dignity which last words imply. Upon the request of a comrade, Gershuni had repeated the substance of the famous speech he had made to the court which sentenced him to Siberia. As representing the government against which he had rebelled, he told the court that he might in time be able to forgive all of their outrages and injustices save one; the unforgivable outrage would remain that hundreds of men like himself, who were vegetarians because they were not willing to participate in the destruction of living creatures, who had never struck a child even in punishment, who were so consumed with tenderness for the outcast and oppressed that they had lived for weeks among starving peasants only that they might cheer and solace them﻿—that these men should have been driven into terrorism, until impelled to “execute,” as they call it﻿—“assassinate” the Anglo-Saxon would term it﻿—public officials, was something for which he would never forgive the Russian government. It was, perhaps, the heat of the argument, as much as conviction, which led me to reply that it would be equally difficult for society to forgive these very revolutionists for one thing they had done, their institution of the use of force in such wise that it would inevitably be imitated by men of less scruple and restraint; that to have revived such a method in civilization, to have justified it by their disinterestedness of purpose and nobility of character, was perhaps the gravest responsibility that any group of men could assume. With a smile of indulgent pity such as one might grant to a mistaken child, he replied that such Tolstoyan principles were as fitted to Russia as “these toilettes,” pointing to the thin summer gowns of his listeners, “were fitted to a Siberian winter.”

===Chinese===

同样，要理解俄国那场伟大的斗争也绝非易事。我记得著名革命者格尔舒尼的来访；他藏在一只卷心菜桶里，就从要塞司令本人的眼皮底下逃出了西伯利亚，经满洲和中国辗转到旧金山，在回俄国的途中在芝加哥停了几天。三个月后我们听到他的死讯；每当我国想同他的谈话，总觉得它蒙上了遗言所舍的那种尊严。应一位同志的请求，格尔舒尼复述了他对判他去西伯利亚的法庭所作那次著名演讲的要点。他对着代表他所反叛的政府的法庭说：他们的暴虐和不义，他假以时日或许都能原谅，唯有一桩不能；那不可原谅的暴虐将永远是：成百个像他这样的人——他们因为不愿参与对生灵的茶毒而吃素，他们连惩罚也没有打过一个孩子，他们对弃民和受压迫者怀着那样灼热的温柔，以致一连几个星期住在饥馑的农民中间，只为给他们打气抚慰——这样一些人竟被驱进了恐怖主义，直到被迫去「处决」——盎格鲁-撒克逊人会称之为「暗杀」——政府官员，这是他永远不能原谅俄国政府的一件事。也许是辩论的火气同信念一样多，使我回答说：社会同样很难原谅这些革命者做的一件事——他们把武力的使用复活到这样的地步，势必被不那么有操守和克制的人模仿；在文明里复活这样一种方法，又用他们动机的无私和品格的高贵来为它辩护，这也许是一群人所能承担的最重大的责任。他带着一种宽容怜惄的微笑，像对一个弄错了的孩子的那种，答道：这样的托尔斯泰式原则之于俄国，就像「这些衣裳」——指着听众们单薄的夏裙——「之于西伯利亚的冬天」。

===Original===

And yet I held the belief then, as I certainly do now, that when the sense of justice seeks to express itself quite outside the regular channels of established government, it has set forth on a dangerous journey inevitably ending in disaster, and that this is true in spite of the fact that the adventure may have been inspired by noble motives.

Still more perplexing than the use of force by the revolutionists is the employment of the agent-provocateur on the part of the Russian government. The visit of Vladimir Bourtzeff to Chicago just after his exposure of the famous secret agent, Azeff, filled one with perplexity in regard to a government which would connive at the violent death of a faithful official and that of a member of the royal household for the sake of bringing opprobrium and punishment to the revolutionists and credit to the secret police.

The Settlement has also suffered through its effort to secure open discussion of the methods of the Russian government. During the excitement connected with the visit of Gorky to this country, three different committees of Russians came to Hull House begging that I would secure a statement in at least one of the Chicago dailies of their own view, that the agents of the Czar had cleverly centered public attention upon Gorky’s private life and had fomented a scandal so successfully that the object of Gorky’s visit to America had been foiled; he who had known intimately the most wretched of the Czar’s subjects, who was best able to sympathetically portray their wretchedness, not only failed to get a hearing before an American audience, but could scarcely find the shelter of a roof. I told two of the Russian committees that it was hopeless to undertake any explanation of the bitter attack until public excitement had somewhat subsided; but one Sunday afternoon when a third committee arrived, I said that I would endeavor to have reprinted in a Chicago daily the few scattered articles written for the magazines which tried to explain the situation, one by the head professor in political economy of a leading university, and others by publicists well informed as to Russian affairs.

===Chinese===

然而我当时抱有、如今当然也抱有这个信念：当正义感试图在既有政府的正规渠道之外表达自己，它就踏上了一段不可避免以灾难告终的危险旅程；哪怕这番冒险受了高尚动机的启发，这一点也不会改变。

比革命者使用武力更令人困惑的，是俄国政府对密探的侦用。弗拉基米尔·布尔采夫揭露了著名密探阿泽夫之后不久访问芝加哥，令人对这样一个政府满心困惑：它竟默许一位忠心官员和一位王室成员死于暴力，只为给革命者带来骂名和惩罚、给秘密警察抬身价。

定居所还因为努力争取公开讨论俄国政府的方法而吃过苦。高尔基访问美国引起的那场风波中，三批不同的俄国人来到赫尔馆，求我至少在芝加哥一家日报上为他们自己的观点登一个声明：沙皇的代理人巧妙地把公众的注意力引到了高尔基的私生活上，煽起一场丑闻，如此成功，以至高尔基访美的目的被挫败；这位最贴身知道沙皇子民中最悲惨者的人，这位最有能力富于同情地描绘他们的悲惨的人，不但没能在美国听众面前得到发言，甚至几乎找不到一片屋顶栖身。我对其中两批俄国委员会说，公众激动平息之前，想为那场苦攻作任何解释都是徒劳；可是有一个星期日下午，第三批委员会来了，我说我愿意设法让一家芝加哥日报转载几篇散见于杂志的试图解释局面的文章——一篇出自一所领头的大学的政治经济学首席教授，另几篇出自熟悉俄国事务的政论家。

===Original===

I hoped that a cosmopolitan newspaper might feel an obligation to recognize the desire for fair play on the part of thousands of its readers among the Russians, Poles, and Finns, at least to the extent of reproducing these magazine articles under a noncommittal caption. That same Sunday evening, in company with one of the residents, I visited a newspaper office only to hear its representative say that my plan was quite out of the question, as the whole subject was what newspaper men called “a sacred cow.” He said, however, that he would willingly print an article which I myself should write and sign. I declined this offer with the statement that one who had my opportunities to see the struggles of poor women in securing support for their children, found it impossible to write anything which would however remotely justify the loosening of marriage bonds, even if the defense of Gorky made by the Russian committees was sound. We left the newspaper office somewhat discouraged with what we thought one more unsuccessful effort to procure a hearing for the immigrants.

I had considered the incident closed, when to my horror and surprise several months afterward it was made the basis of a story with every possible vicious interpretation. One of the Chicago newspapers had been indicted by Mayor Dunne for what he considered an actionable attack upon his appointees to the Chicago School Board of whom I was one, and the incident enlarged and coarsened was submitted as evidence to the Grand Jury in regard to my views and influence. Although the evidence was thrown out, an attempt was again made to revive this story by the managers of Mayor Dunne’s second campaign, this time to show how “the protector of the oppressed” was traduced. The incident is related here as an example of the clever use of that old device which throws upon the radical in religion, in education, and in social reform, the oduim of encouraging “harlots and sinners” and of defending their doctrines.

===Chinese===

我希望一家世界主义的报纸会觉得自己有义务，承认它成千上万的俄国人、波兰人和芬兰读者对公平对待的渴望，至少达到这个程度：用一个不表态的标题转载这些杂志文章。同一天晚上，我同一位居民拜访了一家报馆，只听它的代表说，我的计划完全行不通，因为整个题目是报人所谓的「圣牛」。不过他说，他很愿意登一篇我自己写并署名的文章。我谢绝了这个提议，声明说：一个有我这样的机会、亲眼看着贫穷的妇女们为孩子的抚养挣扎的人，写不出任何哪怕间接为松弛婚姻纽带辩护的东西，哪怕俄国委员会为高尔基作的辩护是对的。我们离开报馆时相当沮丧，以为又是一次为移民争取发言的失败努力。

我以为这件事已经了结；几个月后，合我惊恐和意外，它被编造成一个故事，用上了各种恶毒的演绎。芝加哥一家报纸被邓恩市长指控攻击了他任命的芝加哥教育委员会委员——我也是委员之一；这件事被夸大粗化，当作关于我的观点和影响的证据提交给大陪审团。虽然证据被驳回，邓恩市长第二次竞选的主持者们又试图翻出这个故事，这一次是要显示「受压迫者的保护人」是怎样遭了诽谤。这件事在这里讲出来，是作为那个老手法一个巧用的例证：把「勾结妓女和罪人」、捍卫他们那套学说的骂名，扣到宗教、教育和社会改革里的激进者头上。

===Original===

If the underdog were always right, one might quite easily try to defend him. The trouble is that very often he is but obscurely right, sometimes only partially right, and often quite wrong; but perhaps he is never so altogether wrong and pigheaded and utterly reprehensible as he is represented to be by those who add the possession of prejudices to the other almost insuperable difficulties of understanding him. It was, perhaps, not surprising that with these excellent opportunities for misjudging Hull House, we should have suffered attack from time to time whenever any untoward event gave an opening as when an Italian immigrant murdered a priest in Denver, Colorado. Although the wretched man had never been in Chicago, much less at Hull House, a Chicago ecclesiastic asserted that he had learned hatred of the Church as a member of the Giordano Bruno Club, an Italian Club, one of whose members lived at Hull House, and which had occasionally met there, although it had long maintained clubrooms of its own. This club had its origin in the old struggles of united Italy against the temporal power of the Pope, one of the European echoes with which Chicago resounds. The Italian resident, as the editor of a paper representing new Italy, had come in sharp conflict with the Chicago ecclesiastic, first in regard to naming a public school of the vicinity after Garibaldi, which was of course not tolerated by the Church, and then in regard to many another issue arising in anticlericalism, which, although a political party, is constantly involved, from the very nature of the case, in theological difficulties. The contest had been carried on with a bitterness impossible for an American to understand, but its origin and implications were so obvious that it did not occur to any of us that it could be associated with Hull House either in its motive or direction.

===Chinese===

倘若受欺压者永远正确，人倒可以很容易去保卫他。麻烦在于，他往往只是模糊地正确，有时只是部分正确，又常常完全错误；但他大概从来没有——不像那些人描绘的那样——全盘地错、顽固地蠢、罪无可赦；那些人在理解他的几乎不可克服的困难之外，又添上了成见的占有。赫尔馆有了这些误断的绝好机会，每逢不幸的事件开一道口子，就时不时换一顿攻击——比如一个意大利移民在科罗拉多州丹佛谋杀了一位神父。虽然那个可怜人从未到过芝加哥，更别说赫尔馆，一位芝加哥教士却断言，他是在乔尔丹诺·布鲁诺俱乐部里学会仇恨教会的——那是一个意大利人俱乐部，有一个会员住在赫尔馆，俱乐部偶尔在那里聚会，虽然它早就有了自己的会址。这个俱乐部发源于统一意大利反对教皇世俗权力的旧斗争，是芝加哥回响着的欧洲回声之一。那位意大利居民，作为代表新意大利一家报纸的编辑，同这位芝加哥教士有过尖锐冲突：先是关于以加里波第的名字命名附近一所公立学校——教会当然不能容忍；然后是反教权主义中冒出的许多另一个问题——它虽是一个政党，但从事情的本性出发，不断被卷进神学的麻烦。这场争斗进行得那样苦，美国人无法理解；但它的起因和含义如此明显，我们谁也没想到它能同赫尔馆的动机或方向连在一起。

===Original===

The ecclesiastic himself had lived for years in Rome, and as I had often discussed the problems of Italian politics with him, I was quite sure he understood the raison d’être for the Giordano Bruno Club. Fortunately in the midst of the rhetorical attack, our friendly relations remained unbroken with the neighboring priests from whom we continued to receive uniform courtesy as we cooperated in cases of sorrow and need. Hundreds of devout communicants identified with the various Hull House clubs and classes were deeply distressed by the incident, but assured us it was all a misunderstanding. Easter came soon afterwards, and it was not difficult to make a connection between the attack and the myriad of Easter cards which filled my mail.

Thus a Settlement becomes involved in the many difficulties of its neighbors as its experiences make vivid the consciousness of modern internationalism. And yet the very fact that the sense of reality is so keen and the obligation of the Settlement so obvious may perhaps in itself explain the opposition Hull House has encountered when it expressed its sympathy with the Russian revolution. We were much entertained, although somewhat ruefully, when a Chicago woman withdrew from us a large annual subscription because Hull House had defended a Russian refugee while she, who had seen much of the Russian aristocracy in Europe, knew from them that all the revolutionary agitation was both unreasonable and unnecessary!

It is, of course, impossible to say whether these oppositions were inevitable or whether they were indications that Hull House had somehow bungled at its task. Many times I have been driven to the confession of the blundering Amiel: “It requires ability to make what we seem agree with what we are.”

A view between Hull House Gymnasium and Theater.

===Chinese===

那位教士本人在罗马住过多年，我又常同他讨论意大利政治的问题，我很肯定他懂得乔尔达诺·布鲁诺俱乐部存在的理由。幸而在那场修辞攻击中间，我们同邻近各位神父的友好关系没有破裂；在忧伤和急难的事例中合作时，我们继续从他们那里得到一以贯之的礼遇。同赫尔馆各俱乐部和各班级有关的几百名虔诚信徒，为这件事深感痛苦，但向我们保证这全是误会。不久复活节到了，把这场攻击同塞满我邮箱的无数复活节卡片联系起来，并不困难。

定居所就这样卷进邻里的种种难处，因为它的经历把现代国际主义的意识弄得很鲜活。然而，正是现实感如此敏锐、定居所的义务如此显然这一事实，本身也许就解释了赫尔馆表示同情俄国革命时遭遇的反对。一位芝加哥妇女因为我们保卫了一个俄国难民而撤走了她一笔可观的年度捐款，而她在欧洲见多了俄国贵族，从他们那里知道一切革命鼓动既不合理也无必要——我们听了觉得很好笑，虽然笑得有点苦。

当然，这些反对是不可避免的，还是表明赫尔馆在它的任务上出了差错，无从断言。多少次我被逼到那个跌跌撞撞的亚米伊那样的自白：「让我们显得的东西同我们的本质相合，是需要才能的。」

赫尔馆体操馆与剧场之间的一景。

## XVIII / 十八
===Original===

Socialized Education

In a paper written years ago I deplored at some length the fact that educational matters are more democratic in their political than in their social aspect, and I quote the following extract from it as throwing some light upon the earlier educational undertakings at Hull House:﻿—

“Teaching in a Settlement requires distinct methods, for it is true of people who have been allowed to remain undeveloped and whose facilities are inert and sterile, that they cannot take their learning heavily. It has to be diffused in a social atmosphere, information must be held in solution, in a medium of fellowship and good will.

“Intellectual life requires for its expansion and manifestation the influences and assimilation of the interests and affections of others. Mazzini, that greatest of all democrats, who broke his heart over the condition of the South European peasantry, said: ‘Education is not merely a necessity of true life by which the individual renews his vital force in the vital force of humanity; it is a Holy Communion with generations dead and living, by which he fecundates all his faculties. When he is withheld from this Communion for generations, as the Italian peasant has been, we say, “He is like a beast of the field; he must be controlled by force.” ’ Even to this it is sometimes added that it is absurd to educate him, immoral to disturb his content. We stupidly use the effect as an argument for a continuance of the cause. It is needless to say that a Settlement is a protest against a restricted view of education.”

In line with this declaration, Hull House in the very beginning opened what we called College Extension Classes with a faculty finally numbering thirty-five college men and women, many of whom held their pupils for consecutive years. As these classes antedated in Chicago the University Extension and Normal Extension classes and supplied a demand for stimulating instruction, the attendance strained to their utmost capacity the spacious rooms in the old house. The relation of students and faculty to each other and to the residents was that of guest and hostess, and at the close of each term the residents gave a reception to students and faculty which was one of the chief social events of the season. Upon this comfortable social basis some very good work was done.

===Chinese===

社会化的教育

在一篇多年前写的文章里，我曾相当篇幅地痛惜：教育事务在政治一面比在社会一面更民主。我从那篇文章里引出下面一段，或可照亮赫尔馆早期的教育事业：

「在定居所教书需要独特的方法，因为对于那些被容许一直不曾发展、官能慵懒贫瘠的人，确实不能让他们沉重地接受学习。学问必须在社交的气氛里扩散，信息必须溶在情谊和善意这样一种媒介里。

「心智的生活要扩展要显现，需要他人兴趣和感情的影响与同化。马志尼，一切民主者中最伟大的一位，曾为南欧农民的境况心碎，他说：『教育不仅是真生活的一种必需——个人靠它在人类的生命力里更新自己的生命力；它是一场同死者和生者的圣餐，靠它他使自己的一切官能受孕。当他一代又一代被拒于这场圣餐之外，像意大利农民那样，我们就说：「他像田野的牲口；必须用强力管住他。」』甚至有人在这之外再加一句：教育他是荒唐的，扰得他不得安分是不道德的。我们愚蠢地拿结果当作原因延续下去的论据。不必说，定居所就是对这种狭隘教育观的抗议。」

遵照这一宣言，赫尔馆一开始就开办了我们所谓的高校延伸班（College Extension Classes），师资最后达到三十五名大学生男女，其中许多人把学生一连留了数年。这些班级在芝加哥早于大学延伸班和师范延伸班，又应合着对提神教学的渴求，听讲的人把老屋里宽敞的房间挤到极限。学生和教师彼此之间、同居民之间的关系是客人与女主人；每学期结束时，居民们为学生和教师开一次招待会，那是每季的主要社交事件之一。在这个舒适的社会基础上，做出了一些很好的工作。

===Original===

In connection with these classes a Hull House summer school was instituted at Rockford College, which was most generously placed at our disposal by the trustees. For ten years one hundred women gathered there for six weeks, in addition there were always men on the faculty, and a small group of young men among the students who were lodged in the gymnasium building. The outdoor classes in bird study and botany, the serious reading of literary masterpieces, the boat excursions on the Rock River, the cooperative spirit of doing the housework together, the satirical commencements in parti-colored caps and gowns, lent themselves toward a reproduction of the comradeship which college life fosters.

As each member of the faculty, as well as the students, paid three dollars a week, and as we had little outlay beyond the actual cost of food, we easily defrayed our expenses. The undertaking was so simple and gratifying in results that it might well be reproduced in many college buildings which are set in the midst of beautiful surroundings, unused during the two months of the year when hundreds of people, able to pay only a moderate price for lodgings in the country, can find nothing comfortable and no mental food more satisfying than piazza gossip.

Every Thursday evening during the first years, a public lecture came to be an expected event in the neighborhood, and Hull House became one of the early University Extension centers, first in connection with an independent society and later with the University of Chicago. One of the Hull House trustees was so impressed with the value of this orderly and continuous presentation of economic subjects that he endowed three courses in a downtown center, in which the lectures were free to anyone who chose to come. He was much pleased that these lectures were largely attended by workingmen who ordinarily prefer that an economic subject shall be presented by a partisan, and who are supremely indifferent to examinations and credits. They also dislike the balancing of pro and con which scholarly instruction implies, and prefer to be “inebriated on raw truth” rather than to sip a carefully prepared draught of knowledge.

===Chinese===

与这些班级相联系，赫尔馆在洛克福德学院（Rockford College）办起了暑期学校，院方由理事会慨然交我们使用。十年间，每年一百名妇女在那里聚六个星期；此外师资里总有男性，学生里也总有一小伙青年男子，安置在体操馆楼里。户外的鸟类学和植物学课，文学名著的认真阅读，罗克河上的泛舟，一齐做家务的合作精神，戴着彩帽彩袍的讽剌式毕业典礼——这一切凑成了一种对大学所培育的同志之谊的复现。

由于教师同学生一样每人每周付三块钱，我们在伙食实际成本之外又几乎没有开销，开销很容易就打平了。这项事业如此简单、成效如此可喜，实在不妨在许多大学校舍里复制——它们坐落在优美的环境中，一年里有两个月闲置，而那时成百的人，付得起乡下住宿一个适中的价钱，却找不到什么舒服的住处，也找不到比廊下闲谈更饱人的精神食粮。

头几年里，每逢星期四晚上，一场公开讲演成了街区里被期待的事；赫尔馆成了早期大学延伸中心之一，先依托一个独立会社，后来依托芝加哥大学。赫尔馆的一位理事对这种有秩序、不间断的经济学题目讲授的价值印象如此之深，以致他在市中心一个场所捐设了三门课程，讲演免费，来者不拒。他大为高兴的是，这些讲演大量吸引了工人听众——他们 ordinarily 宁愿经济题目由党派中人来讲，对考试和学分全然无所谓。他们也不喜欢学术讲授所隐含的利弊权衡，宁可「拿生猛的真理把自己灌醉」，不愿细啜一杯精心调制的知识。

===Original===

Nevertheless Bowen Hall, which seats seven hundred and fifty people, is often none too large to hold the audiences of men who come to Hull House every Sunday evening during the winter to attend the illustrated lectures provided by the faculty of the University of Chicago and others who kindly give their services. These courses differ enormously in their popularity: one on European capitals and their social significance was followed with the most vivid attention and sense of participation indicated by groans and hisses when the audience was reminded of an unforgettable feud between Austria and her Slavic subjects, or when they wildly applauded a Polish hero endeared through his tragic failure.

In spite of the success of these Sunday evening courses, it has never been an easy undertaking to find acceptable lectures. A course of lectures on astronomy illustrated by stereopticon slides will attract a large audience the first week, who hope to hear of the wonders of the heavens and the relation of our earth thereto, but instead are treated to spectrum analyses of star dust, or the latest theory concerning the milky way. The habit of research and the desire to say the latest word upon any subject often overcomes the sympathetic understanding of his audience which the lecturer might otherwise develop, and he insensibly drops into the dull terminology of the classroom. There are, of course, notable exceptions; we had twelve gloriously popular talks on organic evolution, but the lecturer was not yet a professor﻿—merely a university instructor﻿—and his mind was still eager over the marvel of it all. Fortunately there is an increasing number of lecturers whose matter is so real, so definite, and so valuable, that in an attempt to give it an exact equivalence in words, they utilize the most direct forms of expression.

===Chinese===

尽管如此，能坐七百五十人的鲍恩厅，在冬天往往还嫌不够大——每星期日晚上都有成群的男子来赫尔馆听图解讲演，由芝加哥大学的教师和其他慨然提供服务的人主讲。这些课程的受欢迎程度大不相同：一门讲欧洲各国首都及其社会意义的课，听众怀着最生动的注意和参与感——一提醒到奥地利同她的斯拉夫臣民间一场难忘的宿怨，满场呻吟嘘声；一位因惨败而更受爱戴的波兰英雄一出场，便狂热鼓掌。

尽管这些星期日晚上的课程很成功，找到受欢迎的讲演却从来不是件容易事。一门用幻灯片图解的天文学课，第一星期会吸引大量听众，他们盼着听天空的奇观和我们地球同它的关系，得到的却是星尘的光谱分析，或关于银河的最新理论。研究的习惯、在任何题目上都要说最新一个字的欲望，常常压倒了讲演者本可培养的对听众的同情理解，他不自觉地滑进课堂上的沉闷术语。当然有显著的例外：我们有过十二场大受欢迎的有机演化讲演，可那位讲演者还不是教授——只是个大学讲师——他的头脑还在为这一切的奇观热切着。幸而越来越多的讲演者，材料如此真实、如此明确、如此有价值，以致在力求用言语给它以确切的对应时，他们用起最直接的表达形式。

===Original===

It sometimes seems as if the men of substantial scholarship were content to leave to the charlatan the teaching of those things which deeply concern the welfare of mankind, and that the mass of men get their intellectual food from the outcasts of scholarship, who provide millions of books, pictures, and shows, not to instruct and guide, but for the sake of their own financial profit. A Settlement soon discovers that simple people are interested in large and vital subjects, and the Hull House residents themselves at one time, with only partial success, undertook to give a series of lectures on the history of the world, beginning with the nebular hypothesis and reaching Chicago itself in the twenty-fifth lecture! Absurd as the hasty review appears, there is no doubt that the beginner in knowledge is always eager for the general statement, as those wise old teachers of the people well knew, when they put the history of creation on the stage and the monks themselves became the actors. I recall that in planning my first European journey I had soberly hoped in two years to trace the entire pattern of human excellence as we passed from one country to another, in the shrines popular affection had consecrated to the saints, in the frequented statues erected to heroes, and in the “worn blasonry of funeral brasses”﻿—an illustration that when we are young we all long for those mountaintops upon which we may soberly stand and dream of our own ephemeral and uncertain attempts at righteousness. I have had many other illustrations of this; a statement was recently made to me by a member of the Hull House Boys’ club, who had been unjustly arrested as an accomplice to a young thief and held in the police station for three days, that during his detention he “had remembered the way Jean Valjean behaved when he was everlastingly pursued by that policeman who was only trying to do right”; “I kept seeing the pictures in that illustrated lecture you gave about him, and I thought it would be queer if I couldn’t behave well for three days when he had kept it up for years.”

===Chinese===

有时候仿佛是：学问扎实的学者们甘愿把那些深切关系人类福祉的东西的讲授留给走江湖的骗子，而大众的智力食粮来自学问的弃儿——他们提供千百万本书、画片和演出，不为教导指引，只为自己的财利。定居所很快发现，淳朴的人对宏大而切身的题目感兴趣；赫尔馆的居民们自己就一度——只成功了一半——开设过一系列世界史讲演，从星云假说讲起，到第二十五讲才讲到芝加哥！这匆促的概观虽然显得荒唐，毫无疑问，知识上的初学者永远渴望总括的陈述，正如人民那些智慧的老教师所深知的——他们把创世的历史搬上舞台，让修士们亲自充当演员。我记得我筹划第一次欧洲之行时，曾郑重希望在两年里描出人类优异的整个图样：从一国到另一国，在民间感情献给圣徒的圣祠里，在常有人瞻仰的英雄雕像里，在「葬铜牌上磨旧的纹章」里——这说明年轻时我们都渴望那些山顶，好站在上面梦想自己那短暂而无常的向善努力。这样的例证我还有许多；赫尔馆男孩俱乐部一个会员最近对我说，他因为被误作一个小偷的同伙、不公正地被捕，在警察局拘了三天，拘押期间「一直记得冉·阿让被那个一心只想尽职的警察永远追捕时是怎么做人的」；「我一直想着你讲他时那场图解讲演里的画面；他坚持了那么多年，我要是连三天都撑不住，那才怪呢。」

===Original===

The power of dramatic action may unfortunately be illustrated in other ways. During the weeks when all the daily papers were full of the details of a notorious murder trial in New York and all the hideous events which preceded the crime, one evening I saw in the street a knot of working girls leaning over a newspaper, admiring the clothes, the beauty, and “sorrowful expression” of the unhappy heroine. In the midst of the trial a woman whom I had known for years came to talk to me about her daughter, shamefacedly confessing that the girl was trying to dress and look like the notorious girl in New York, and that she had even said to her mother in a moment of defiance, “Some day I shall be taken into court and then I shall dress just as Evelyn did and face my accusers as she did in innocence and beauty.”

If one makes calls on a Sunday afternoon in the homes of the immigrant colonies near Hull House, one finds the family absorbed in the Sunday edition of a sensational daily newspaper, even those who cannot read, quite easily following the comic adventures portrayed in the colored pictures of the supplement or tracing the clue of a murderer carefully depicted by a black line drawn through a plan of the houses and streets.

===Chinese===

戏剧性行为的力量，不幸也可以用别的方式例证。那几个星期里，各日报登满了纽约一桩轰动的谋杀审判以及罪行之前种种可憎事件的细节；一天晚上，我在街上看见一小群职业少女俯在一张报纸上，赞赏那不幸女主角的衣裳、美貌和「哀愁的神情」。审判期间，一位我认识多年的妇女来同我谈她的女儿，面带羞地承认：那姑娘一心想穿得、打扮得像纽约那个出名的姑娘，甚至有一回倔头倔脑地对母亲说：「总有一天我会被带上法庭，那时候我就穿得同伊夫琳一模一样，像她那样无辜而美丽地面质控告我的人。」

星期日下午若到赫尔馆附近移民聚居区的家里走访，会发现全家沉浸在一份数感日报的星期日版里；连不识字的人也毫不费劲地跟着副刊彩图里连环滑稽冒险走，或者循着那条小心画出的黑线——它穿过一幅房屋和街道的平面图——追踪凶手的线索。

===Original===

Sometimes lessons in the great loyalties and group affections come through life itself and yet in such a manner that one cannot but deplore it. During the teamsters’ strike in Chicago several years ago when class bitterness rose to a dramatic climax, I remember going to visit a neighborhood boy who had been severely injured when he had taken the place of a union driver upon a coal wagon. As I approached the house in which he lived, a large group of boys and girls, some of them very little children, surrounded me to convey the exciting information that “Jack T. was a scab,” and that I couldn’t go in there. I explained to the excited children that his mother, who was a friend of mine, was in trouble, quite irrespective of the way her boy had been hurt. The crowd around me outside of the house of the “scab” constantly grew larger and I, finally abandoning my attempt at explanation, walked in only to have the mother say: “Please don’t come here. You will only get hurt, too.” Of course I did not get hurt, but the episode left upon my mind one of the most painful impressions I have ever received in connection with the children of the neighborhood. In addition to all else are the lessons of loyalty and comradeship to come to them as the mere reversals of class antagonism? And yet it was but a trifling incident out of the general spirit of bitterness and strife which filled the city.

===Chinese===

有时，大忠诚和大群体感情的课程来自生活本身，而且来的方式叫人不能不痛惜。几年前芝加哥货车夫罢工、阶级怨恨升到戏剧性顶点的时候，我记得去看望一个街区的男孩，他因为顶替一个工会司机赶煤车受了重伤。我走近他住的房子时，一大群男孩女孩——有的还是非常小的孩子——围住我，传递那个激动人心的消息：「杰克·T是个工贼」，说我不能进去。我向激动的孩子们解释：他母亲是我的朋友，正遭着难，这同她儿子受伤的缘由毫不相干。「工贼」家门外围着我的人越聚越多；我终于放弃了讲解的尝试，径直走进去，只听那位母亲说：「请您别来这儿。您只会也挨打。」我当然没有挨打，但这件事在我心里留下的，是我同街区孩子们有关的一切印象中最痛的一种。忠诚与伙伴之情，竟以阶级对抗的单纯反转来到他们身上？——在这一切之上还要加上这个吗？而这也只是充塞全城的那股怨恨与争斗的一般气氛里一件小小的事故。

===Original===

Therefore the residents of Hull House place increasing emphasis upon the great inspirations and solaces of literature and are unwilling that it should ever languish as a subject for class instruction or for reading parties. The Shakespeare club has lived a continuous existence at Hull House for sixteen years during which time its members have heard the leading interpreters of Shakespeare, both among scholars and players. I recall that one of its earliest members said that her mind was peopled with Shakespeare characters during her long hours of sewing in a shop, that she couldn’t remember what she thought about before she joined the club, and concluded that she hadn’t thought about anything at all. To feed the mind of the worker, to lift it above the monotony of his task, and to connect it with the larger world, outside of his immediate surroundings, has always been the object of art, perhaps never more nobly fulfilled than by the great English bard. Miss Starr has held classes in Dante and Browning for many years, and the great lines are conned with never failing enthusiasm. I recall Miss Lathrop’s Plato club and an audience who listened to a series of lectures by Dr. John Dewey on “Social Psychology” as geniune intellectual groups consisting largely of people from the immediate neighborhood, who were willing to make “that effort from which we all shrink, the effort of thought.” But while we prize these classes as we do the help we are able to give to the exceptional young man or woman who reaches the college and university and leaves the neighborhood of his childhood behind him, the residents of Hull House feel increasingly that the educational efforts of a Settlement should not be directed primarily to reproduce the college type of culture, but to work out a method and an ideal adapted to the immediate situation. They feel that they should promote a culture which will not set its possessor aside in a class with others like himself, but which will, on the contrary, connect him with all sorts of people by his ability to understand them as well as by his power to supplement their present surroundings with the historic background.

===Chinese===

因此，赫尔馆的居民们越来越着重文学伟大的激励与抚慰，不愿它在课堂讲授或读书会里渐渐失去生气。莎士比亚俱乐部在赫尔馆连续存在了十六年，其间会员们听过阐释莎士比亚的顶尖人物——学者中的和演员中的。我记得它最早的一位会员说，在店里做针线的漫长钟点里，她头脑里住满了莎士比亚的人物；她记不起入会之前都想些什么，结论是从前根本什么也没想。喂养劳动者的头脑，把它从活计的单调里提升起来，把它同眼前环境之外更大的世界连接起来，一向是艺术的目的，也许从来没有比伟大的英国吟游诗人完成得更崇高的。斯塔尔小姐（Miss Starr）多年主持但丁和勃朗宁的班，伟大的诗行被一遍遍诵读，热忱从不稍减。我记得拉斯罗普小姐的柏拉图俱乐部，记得听约翰·杜威博士（Dr. John Dewey）讲「社会心理学」系列的一群听众——都是地道的智识团体，成员大半来自紧邻街区，愿意付出「那人人都畏缩的努力——思考的努力」。然而，我们珍视这些班级，正如珍视我们能给那例外地升上大学、把童年的街区留在身后的青年男女的帮助；赫尔馆的居民们却越来越觉得：定居所的教育努力，首先不该去复制学院型的文化，而该去找出一种适应眼前处境的方法和理想。他们觉得应该促成这样一种文化：它不把拥有者安置在一个同他相似者组成的阶级里，反而凭着理解形形色色人的能力、凭着用历史背景补足他们眼前环境的能力，把他同一切人连接起来。

===Original===

Among the hundreds of immigrants who have for years attended classes at Hull House designed primarily to teach the English language, dozens of them have struggled to express in the newly acquired tongue some of these hopes and longings which had so much to do with their emigration.

A series of plays was thus written by a young Bohemian; essays by a Russian youth, outpouring sorrows rivaling Werther himself and yet containing the precious stuff of youth’s perennial revolt against accepted wrong; stories of Russian oppression and petty injustices throughout which the desire for free America became a crystallized hope; an attempt to portray the Jewish day of Atonement, in such wise that even individualistic Americans may catch a glimpse of that deeper national life which has survived all transplanting and expresses itself in forms so ancient that they appear grotesque to the ignorant spectator. I remember a pathetic effort on the part of a young Russian Jewess to describe the vivid inner life of an old Talmud scholar, probably her uncle or father, as of one persistently occupied with the grave and important things of the spirit, although when brought into sharp contact with busy and overworked people, he inevitably appeared self-absorbed and slothful. Certainly no one who had read her paper could again see such an old man in his praying shawl bent over his crabbed book, without a sense of understanding.

On the other hand, one of the most pitiful periods in the drama of the much-praised young American who attempts to rise in life, is the time when his educational requirements seem to have locked him up and made him rigid. He fancies himself shut off from his uneducated family and misunderstood by his friends. He is bowed down by his mental accumulations and often gets no farther than to carry them through life as a great burden, and not once does he obtain a glimpse of the delights of knowledge.

===Chinese===

多年来在赫尔馆上课——课的首要目的是教英语——的成百移民中，有几十人挣扎着用这新学的语言，表达那些同他们移民关系极大的希望与渴望。

一个年轻的波希米亚人就这样写了一个系列剧；一个俄国青年写了散文，倾吐的愁苦堪比维特本人，却含有青年对公认的过错永恒反叛的宝贵质料；还有俄国压迫和种种小不公的故事，字里行间，对自由美国的渴望凝成了结晶的希望；还有一次对犹太赎罪日的描摹，连个人主义的美国人也能从中瞥见那更深的国家生活——它历尽移植而存活，用古老得让无知的旁观者觉得怪诞的形式表达自己。我记得一个年轻的俄国犹太姑娘一次动人的努力：描写一位老年塔木德学者生动的内心生活——多半是她的伯叔或父亲——一个执着于精神里那些重大之事的人；可是一旦同忙碌操劳的人作尖锐的接触，他就难免显得自我专注而怠惰。读过她那篇文章的人，再见这样的老人披着祈祷巾、俯在他的艰涩书本上，一定不会不带着一份了解。

另一方面，那位备受称赏、想要往上走的美国青年，他的戏里最堪悲悯的一段，是他的教育要求仿佛把他锁住、弄得他僵直的时期。他想象自己同没受过教育的家庭隔绝了，被朋友们误解了。他被自己的心智积累压弯，往往一辈子背着这份重担走，别无所得，连知识之乐的一瞥也不曾得到。

===Original===

The teacher in a Settlement is constantly put upon his mettle to discover methods of instruction which shall make knowledge quickly available to his pupils, and I should like here to pay my tribute of admiration to the dean of our educational department, Miss Landsberg, and to the many men and women who every winter come regularly to Hull House, putting untiring energy into the endless task of teaching the newly arrived immigrant the first use of a language of which he has such desperate need. Even a meager knowledge of English may mean an opportunity to work in a factory versus nonemployment, or it may mean a question of life or death when a sharp command must be understood in order to avoid the danger of a descending crane.

In response to a demand for an education which should be immediately available, classes have been established and grown apace in cooking, dressmaking, and millinery. A girl who attends them will often say that she “expects to marry a workingman next spring,” and because she has worked in a factory so long she knows “little about a house.” Sometimes classes are composed of young matrons of like factory experiences. I recall one of them whose husband had become so desperate after two years of her unskilled cooking that he had threatened to desert her and go where he could get “decent food,” as she confided to me in a tearful interview, when she followed my advice to take the Hull House courses in cooking, and at the end of six months reported a united and happy home.

Two distinct trends are found in response to these classes; the first is for domestic training, and the other is for trade teaching which shall enable the poor little milliner and dressmaker apprentices to shorten the years of errand running which is supposed to teach them their trade.

===Chinese===

定居所的教师时时被逼出全部本领，去发现能使学生迅速受用的教学方法；我在这里愿向我们教育部门的主任兰茨贝格小姐（Miss Landsberg）致敬，也向每年冬天按时来赫尔馆的许多男女致敬——他们把不知疲倦的精力投进那无穷无尽的任务：教新到的移民初步使用他急需的语言。哪怕贫乏的一点英语，也可能意味着进厂做工的机会对失业，也可能意味着生死：一声急促的口令必须听懂，才能躲开吊车降下的危险。

为回应一种必须立即可用的教育的要求，烹调、裁剪、女帽的班级办起来并迅速扩大。来上课的姑娘常说她「指望明年春天嫁给一个工人」，又因为在工厂做了那么久，她「不大僅得家事」。有时班级由经历相仿的年轻主妇组成。我记得其中一个：她两年不会做饭，丈夫绝望到威胁要抛下她，去能吃上「像样饭食」的地方——她含泪见我时吐露的；她听了我的劝告上赫尔馆的烹调课，六个月后来报告：一个团聚而快乐的家。

这些班招来了两种分明的趋向：一是家政训练，一是行业教学——好让可怜的小女帽学徒和裁缝学徒缩短跑腿的年头，那跑腿本该教会她们手艺。

===Original===

The beginning of trade instruction has been already evolved in connection with the Hull House Boys’ club. The ample Boys’ club building presented to Hull House three years ago by one of our trustees has afforded well-equipped shops for work in wood, iron, and brass; for smithing in copper and tin; for commercial photography, for printing, for telegraphy, and electrical construction. These shops have been filled with boys who are eager for that which seems to give them a clue to the industrial life all about them. These classes meet twice a week and are taught by intelligent workingmen who apparently give the boys what they want better than do the strictly professional teachers. While these classes in no sense provide a trade training, they often enable a boy to discover his aptitude and help him in the selection of what he “wants to be” by reducing the trades to embryonic forms. The factories are so complicated that the boy brought in contact with them, unless he has some preliminary preparation, is apt to become confused. In pedagogical terms, he loses his “power of orderly reaction” and is often so discouraged or so overstimulated in his very first years of factory life that his future usefulness is seriously impaired.

One of Chicago’s most significant experiments in the direction of correlating the schools with actual industry was for several years carried on in a public school building situated near Hull House, in which the bricklayers’ apprentices were taught eight hours a day in special classes during the non-bricklaying season. This early public school venture anticipated the very successful arrangement later carried on in Cincinnati, in Pittsburgh and in Chicago itself, whereby a group of boys at work in a factory alternate month by month with another group who are in school and are thus intelligently conducted into the complicated processes of modern industry. But for a certain type of boy who has been demoralized by the constant change and excitement of street life, even these apprenticeship classes are too strenuous, and he has to be lured into the path of knowledge by all sorts of appeals.

===Chinese===

行业教学的发端已经在赫尔馆男孩俱乐部里长出来。三年前我们的一位理事赠给赫尔馆的那座宽敞的男孩俱乐部楼，为木工、铁工、黄铜工，为铜、锡锻打，为商业摄影、印刷、电报和电气装配提供了设备良好的工场。这些工场挤满了男孩，他们渴望那仿佛能给他们一把理解四周工业生活之钥的东西。这些班每星期聚两次，由聪明的工人执教，他们显然比严格科班的教师更给得出男孩们想要的东西。这些班虽然谈不上提供行业训练，却常使男孩发现自己的天资，帮他在各行各业被还原为胚胎形态之后，选定自己「要做什么」。工厂如此复杂，同它接触的男孩若没有一点预备，很容易变糊涂。用教育学的话说，他丧失了「有序反应的能力」；在工厂生活的头几年里就深受挫败或过度受激，以致未来的用处受到严重损害。

使学校同实际工业相衔接，芝加哥最有意义的实验之一，一连几年就在赫尔馆附近一座公立校舍里进行：砸砖学徒们在非砸砖季节里，每天在特殊班级里上课八小时。这项早期的公立学校尝试，预示了后来在辛辛那提、匹兹堡和芝加哥本身办得很成功的安排：在工厂做工的一组男孩按月同在学校的另一组轮换，就这样被聪明地引进现代工业的复杂过程。可是对某一类被街头生活的变动和刺激败坏了的男孩，连这些学徒班也太紧了，得用种种诱饵把他诱上知识之路。

===Original===

It sometimes happens that boys are held in the Hull House classes for weeks by their desire for the excitement of placing burglar alarms under the door mats. But to enable the possessor of even a little knowledge to thus play with it, is to decoy his feet at least through the first steps of the long, hard road of learning, although even in this, the teacher must proceed warily. A typical street boy who was utterly absorbed in a woodcarving class, abruptly left never to return when he was told to use some simple calculations in the laying out of the points. He evidently scented the approach of his old enemy, arithmetic, and fled the field. On the other hand, we have come across many cases in which boys have vainly tried to secure such opportunities for themselves. During the trial of a boy of ten recently arrested for truancy, it developed that he had spent many hours watching the electrical construction in a downtown building, and many others in the public library “reading about electricity.” Another boy who was taken from school early, when his father lost both of his legs in a factory accident, tried in vain to find a place for himself “with machinery.” He was declared too small for any such position, and for four years worked as an errand boy, during which time he steadily turned in his unopened pay envelope for the use of the household. At the end of the fourth year the boy disappeared, to the great distress of his invalid father and his poor mother whose day washings became the sole support of the family. He had beaten his way to Kansas City, hoping “they wouldn’t be so particular there about a fellow’s size.” He came back at the end of six weeks because he felt sorry for his mother who, aroused at last to a realization of his unbending purpose, applied for help to the Juvenile Protective Association. They found a position for the boy in a machine shop and an opportunity for evening classes.

===Chinese===

有时男孩们被想在门垫底下装防盗铃的兴奋搜住，一连几个星期不肯离开赫尔馆的班。可是让哪怕只拥有一点点知识的人这样玩弄它，至少也是把他的脚步诱进学习那条漫长艰辛之路的头几步——虽然连在这上头，教师也须走得小心。一个典型的街头男孩，本来完全沉浸在木雕班里，一被要求在定点位时用些简单的计算，突然一去不回。他显然嗅到了老仇人算术的临近，逃离了战场。另一方面，我们遇到过许多男孩自己徒然寻这样的机会的例子。一个十岁男孩最近因逃学受审，审出他在市中心一座建筑的电气装配前看了许多个钟头，又在公共图书馆里消磨了许多个，「读电的东西」。另一个男孩早早离开了学校——他父亲在工厂事故里失去了双腿——想给自己找一个「同机器在一起」的位置，却徒劳无功。人家说他个子太小，什么这样的位置都不行；他做了四年跑腿的，四年里把原封未拆的工资袋源源上交家用。第四年末男孩失踪了，苦了他的伤残父亲和他那可怜的母亲——她替人白日洗衣成了全家唯一的依靠。他一路白坐车到了堪萨斯城，指望「那边对一个人的个子不会那么挑剧」。六个星期后他回来了，因为他心疼母亲；母亲终于认清了他不弯的目的，向少年保护协会求助。他们给男孩在一家机械厂找到了位置，也找到了上夜校的机会。

===Original===

Out of the fifteen hundred members of the Hull House Boy’s club, hundreds seem to respond only to the opportunities for recreation, and many of the older ones apparently care only for the bowling and the billiards. And yet tournaments and match games under supervision and regulated hours are a great advance over the sensual and exhausting pleasures to be found so easily outside the club. These organized sports readily connect themselves with the Hull House gymnasium and with all those enthusiasms which are so mysteriously aroused by athletics.

Our gymnasium has been filled with large and enthusiastic classes for eighteen years in spite of the popularity of dancing and other possible substitutes, while the Saturday evening athletic contests have become a feature of the neighborhood. The Settlement strives for that type of gymnastics which is at least partly a matter of character, for that training which presupposes abstinence and the curbing of impulse, as well as for those athletic contests in which the mind of the contestant must be vigilant to keep the body closely to the rules of the game. As one sees in rhythmic motion the slim bodies of a class of lads, “that scrupulous and uncontaminate purity of form which recommended itself even to the Greeks as befitting messengers from the gods, if such messengers should come,” one offers up in awkward prosaic form the very essence of that old prayer, “Grant them with feet so light to pass through life.” But while the glory stored up for Olympian winners was at the most a handful of parsley, an ode, fame for family and city, on the other hand, when the men and boys from the Hull House gymnasium bring back their cups and medals, one’s mind is filled with something like foreboding in the reflection that too much success may lead the winners into the professionalism which is so associated with betting and so close to pugilism. Candor, however, compels me to state that a long acquaintance with the acrobatic folk who have to do with the circus, a large number of whom practice in our gymnasium every winter, has raised our estimate of that profession.

===Chinese===

赫尔馆男孩俱乐部的一千五百名会员里，几百人似乎只响应娱乐的机会，年长些的许多显然只关心保龄球和台球。然而，有监督、限钟点的锦标赛和对抗赛，比起俱乐部外轻易可得的那些纵欲而耗人的快乐，终究是长足的进步。这些有组织的运动很容易同赫尔馆体操馆连接，同体育神秘地激发的一切热忱连接。

十八年来，尽管跳舞和其他可能的替代如此流行，我们的体操馆一直挤满了庞大而热烈的班级；星期六晚上的运动竞赛已经成了街区的一景。定居所追求那样一种体操——它至少部分是品格的事；那样一种训练——它以节制和约束冲动为前提；也追求那样一种竞赛——参赛者的头脑必须警惕，使身体紧守比赛规则。看着一班少年细长的身躯在律动中，看见「那甚至在希腊人看来也宜于充当神之信使的、如此审慎而不受沾染的形式纯粹——倘若这样的信使会来的话」，人便以笨拙的散文形式献上那古老祷词的精髄：「赐他们以如此轻盈的双脚走过一生。」可是，奥林匹克优胜者积攒的荣光至多是一把芹葉、一首颂歌、家族和城邦的名声；另一方面，当赫尔馆体操馆的男子和男孩捧回他们的奖杯和奖牌，人心里的却近乎不祥的预感：太大的成功也许会把优胜者引进那种同打赌关系太深、离拳击太近的职业化。不过，坦白也逼我说一句：同杂技艺人们——他们同马戏有关，每年冬天许多人在我们体操馆练习——长久相识，提高了我们对那一行的评价。

===Original===

Young people who work long hours at sedentary occupations, factories and offices, need perhaps more than anything else the freedom and ease to be acquired from a symmetrical muscular development and are quick to respond to that fellowship which athletics apparently affords more easily than anything else. The Greek immigrants form large classes and are eager to reproduce the remnants of old methods of wrestling, and other bits of classic lore which they still possess, and when one of the Greeks won a medal in a wrestling match which represented the championship of the entire city, it was quite impossible that he should present it to the Hull House trophy chest without a classic phrase which he recited most gravely and charmingly.

It was in connection with a large association of Greek lads that Hull House finally lifted its long restriction against military drill. If athletic contests are the residuum of warfare first waged against the conqueror without and then against the tyrants within the State, the modern Greek youth is still in the first stage so far as his inherited attitude against the Turk is concerned. Each lad believes that at any moment he may be called home to fight this longtime enemy of Greece. With such a genuine motive at hand, it seemed mere affectation to deny the use of our boys’ club building and gymnasium for organized drill, although happily it forms but a small part of the activities of the Greek Educational Association.

===Chinese===

在工厂和写字间久坐劳作的年轻人，也许比什么都更需要从匀称的肌肉发展中获得自由与舒展，也最容易响应体育显然比什么都容易提供的那种伙伴之情。希腊移民组成庞大的班级，渴望重现旧式摔跤的残存，以及他们仍保存的其他古典知识零片；当一个希腊人赢得一枚代表全市冠军的摔跤奖牌时，要他不伴一句古典的言辞就把它献进赫尔馆的奖品柜，是完全不可能的——他背得庄重而动人。

正是同一个大型希腊少年联合会打之道，赫尔馆终于解除了对军事操练的长期禁令。倘若运动竞赛是战争的残余——先是对外来征服者、后是对城内僭主打的那场——那么现代希腊青年，就他对土耳其人的世袭敌位而言，还处在第一阶段。每个少年都相信，他随时可能被召回家去同希腊的这个宿敌作战。手头有这样真实的动机，再不许男孩俱乐部楼和体操馆用于有组织的操练，似乎只是装模作样；幸而它只占希腊教育协会（Greek Educational Association）活动的一小部分。

===Original===

Having thus confessed to military drill countenanced if not encouraged at Hull House, it is perhaps only fair to relate an early experience of mine with the “Columbian Guards,” and organization of the World’s Fair summer. Although the Hull House squad was organized as the others were with the motto of a clean city, it was very anxious for military drill. This request not only shocked my nonresistant principles, but seemed to afford an opportunity to find a substitute for the military tactics which were used in the boys’ brigades everywhere, even in those connected with churches. As the cleaning of the filthy streets and alleys was the ostensible purpose of the Columbian guards, I suggested to the boys that we work out a drill with sewer spades, which with their long narrow blades and shortened handles were not so unlike bayoneted guns in size, weight, and general appearance, but that much of the usual military drill could be readapted. While I myself was present at the gymnasium to explain that it was nobler to drill in imitation of removing disease-breeding filth than to drill in simulation of warfare; while I distractedly readapted tales of chivalry to this modern rescuing of the endangered and distressed, the new drill went forward in some sort of fashion, but so surely as I withdrew, the drillmaster would complain that our troops would first grow self-conscious, then demoralized, and finally flatly refuse to go on. Throughout the years since the failure of this quixotic experiment, I occasionally find one of these sewer spades in a Hull House storeroom, too truncated to be used for its original purpose and too prosaic to serve the purpose for which it was bought. I can only look at it in the forlorn hope that it may foreshadow that piping time when the weapons of warfare shall be turned into the implements of civic salvation.

Before closing this chapter on socialized education, it is only fair to speak of the education accruing to the Hull House residents themselves during their years of living in what at least purports to be a center for social and educational activity.

===Chinese===

既然招认了赫尔馆容忍（如果不是鼓励）军事操练，或许也该公平地讲一件我早年同「哥伦布卫队」的经历——那是世博会夏天的一个组织。赫尔馆的小队同别处的一样，以清洁城市为箴言，却一心想着军事操练。这请求不但震悚了我的不抵抗主义原则，也似乎提供了一个机会，去给各地少年旅——连教会办的也不例外——用的军事战术找个替代。既然清扫肮脏街巷是哥伦布卫队表面上的目的，我便向男孩们提议用下水道铲操练：铲子长窄的刃加短了的柄，在大小、分量和总体外观上同上了刺刀的枪没有那么不同，寻常的军事操练大半可以改用。我自己到体操馆去解释：模仿清除生疾的污秽比模仿作战更崇高；我心不在焉地把骑士传奇改写成对危难者受苦者的现代营救——新操练就这样勉勉强强进行了下去；可是我一抽身，教官就来抱怨：我们的部队先变得扭捉，继而涣散，最后干脆拒绝继续。这场堂吉诃德式实验失败之后的这些年里，我偶尔还会在赫尔馆的贮物间里发现一把这样的下水道铲，截得太短，本来的用途用不上了，又太平淡，配不上买它的目的。我只能对着它抱一线孤希望：它也许预示着那个太平岁月——作战的武器将要锻打成为公民救腹的工具。

在结束这章社会化的教育之前，还该公平地讲一讲赫尔馆居民们自己在这座至少号称是社会与教育活动中心的房子里居住多年间所获得的教育。

===Original===

While a certain number of the residents are primarily interested in charitable administration and the amelioration which can be suggested only by those who know actual conditions, there are other residents identified with the House from its earlier years to whom the groups of immigrants make the historic appeal, and who use, not only their linguistic ability, but all the resource they can command of travel and reading to qualify themselves for intelligent living in the immigrant quarter of the city. I remember one resident lately returned from a visit in Sicily, who was able to interpret to a bewildered judge the ancient privilege of a jilted lover to scratch the cheek of his faithless sweetheart with the edge of a coin. Although the custom in America had degenerated into a knife slashing after the manner of foreign customs here, and although the Sicilian deserved punishment, the incident was yet lifted out of the slough of mere brutal assault, and the interpretation won the gratitude of many Sicilians.

There is no doubt that residents in a Settlement too often move toward their ends “with hurried and ignoble gait,” putting forth thorns in their eagerness to bear grapes. It is always easy for those in pursuit of ends which they consider of overwhelming importance to become themselves thin and impoverished in spirit and temper, to gradually develop a dark mistaken eagerness alternating with fatigue, which supersedes “the great and gracious ways” so much more congruous with worthy aims.

Partly because of this universal tendency, partly because a Settlement shares the perplexities of its times and is never too dogmatic concerning the final truth, the residents would be glad to make the daily life at the Settlement “conform to every shape and mode of excellence.”

It may not be true

“That the good are always the merry

Save by an evil chance,”

but a Settlement would make clear that one need not be heartless and flippant in order to be merry, nor solemn in order to be wise. Therefore quite as Hull House tries to redeem billiard tables from the association of gambling, and dancing from the temptations of the public dance halls, so it would associate with a life of upright purpose those more engaging qualities which in the experience of the neighborhood are too often connected with dubious aims.

===Chinese===

一部分居民主要关心慈善管理和改善——那只有知道实际情况的人才能献策；另一些同馆相始终的早期居民，则被一群体体的移民作了历史的召唤，他们不但用自己的语言能力，还用旅行和阅读所能调动的一切资源，来使自己胜任在城市移民区的明智生活。我记得一位居民刚从西西里访问回来，能向一位惶惑的法官诠释被负心姑娘的旧日特权：用硬币的边缘搔划她的脸。虽然这风俗在美国已退化为照例外国风俗在此退化的那样——动刀子；虽然那西西里人应受惩罚，这件事毕竟从单纯粗暴伤害的泥潭里被提了出来，这份诠释赢得许多西西里人的感激。

毫无疑问，定居所里的居民奔向自己的目的时，太常「步履匆忙而卑琙」，急于结葡萄，先伸出了荆棘。追求自认压倒一切之目的的人，最容易自己在精神和脾气上变得单薄和贫乏，渐渐生出一种同疲惫交替的、阴暗而错误的急切，取代了那与可称的目标相称得多的「伟大而仁厚的方式」。

一部分因为这种普遍的倾向，一部分因为定居所分担着时代的困惑、对终极真理从不那么独断，居民们乐于使定居所的日常生活「符合一切卓越的形状与样式」。

「善良的人永远快活／除非遇了恶运」，这话也许不真；可是定居所要澄清：人不必为了快活就无心肝轻儇，也不必为了智慧就板起面孔。因此，正像赫尔馆努力把台球桌从赌博的勾连里赎出来、把跳舞从公共舞厅的诱惑里赎出来，它也要把那些更有吸引力的品质——在街区的经验里它们太常同可疑的目的连在一起——同正直目的的生活连结起来。

===Original===

Throughout the history of Hull House many inquiries have been made concerning the religion of the residents, and the reply that they are as diversified in belief and in the ardor of the inner life as any like number of people in a college or similar group, apparently does not carry conviction. I recall that after a house for men residents had been opened on Polk Street and the residential force at Hull House numbered twenty, we made an effort to come together on Sunday evenings in a household service, hoping thus to express our moral unity in spite of the fact that we represented many creeds. But although all of us reverently knelt when the High Church resident read the evening service and bowed our heads when the evangelical resident led in prayer after his chapter, and although we sat respectfully through the twilight when a resident read her favorite passages from Plato and another from Abt Vogler, we concluded at the end of the winter that this was not religious fellowship and that we did not care for another reading club. So it was reluctantly given up, and we found that it was quite as necessary to come together on the basis of the deed and our common aim inside the household as it was in the neighborhood itself. I once had a conversation on the subject with the warden of Oxford House, who kindly invited me to the evening service held for the residents in a little chapel on the top floor of the Settlement. All the residents were High Churchmen to whom the service was an important and reverent part of the day. Upon my reply to a query of the warden that the residents of Hull House could not come together for religious worship because there were among us Jews, Roman Catholics, English Churchmen, Dissenters, and a few agnostics, and that we had found unsatisfactory the diluted form of worship which we could carry on together, he replied that it must be most difficult to work with a group so diversified, for he depended upon the evening service to clear away any difficulties which the day had involved and to bring the residents to a religious consciousness of their common aim.

===Chinese===

赫尔馆的全部历史上，不断有人来询问居民们的宗教；回答是：他们像大学或类似团体里任何同样数目的人一样，信仰和内心生活热忱各异——这回答显然不能服人。我记得波尔克街的男性居民楼开办、赫尔馆居民达到二十人之后，我们努力在星期日晚上聚起来行一次家内礼拜，希望以此表达我们的道德一体，尽管我们代表许多信条。虽然高教会派居民诵晚祷时我们全都虔敬跪下，福音派居民读完他那章后领祷时我们一齐低头，虽然一位居民读她心爱的柏拉图段落、另一位读《阿卜特·沃格勒》时我们恭敬地坐到暮色四合，一冬下来我们断定：这不是宗教的情谊，我们也不要再办一个读书会。于是勉强放弃了；我们发现，在行动和我们共同目标的基础上聚到一起，同在街区里一样必要。一次我就这个题目同牛津馆（Oxford House）的馆长谈话，他好心请我参加为居民们在定居所顶层小礼拜堂举行的晚祷。居民们全是高教会派，对他们，那礼拜是一天里重要而虔敬的一部分。馆长问我，我答说赫尔馆的居民们不能聚在一起礼拜，因为我们中间有犹太人、罗马天主教徒、英国国教徒、不顺从国教者，还有几个不可知论者；我们一起所能举行的那种稀释了的礼拜，我们发现不能令人满意。他答说，同这么驳杂的一群人一起工作想必极难，因为全靠晚祷来清除白天卷入的种种困难、把居民们带到对共同目标的宗教意识里。

===Original===

I replied that this diversity of creed was part of the situation in American Settlements, as it was our task to live in a neighborhood of many nationalities and faiths, and that it might be possible that among such diversified people it was better that the Settlement corps should also represent varying religious beliefs.

A wise man has told us that “men are once for all so made that they prefer a rational world to believe in and to live in,” but that it is no easy matter to find a world rational as to its intellectual, aesthetic, moral, and practical aspects. Certainly it is no easy matter if the place selected is of the very sort where the four aspects are apparently furthest from perfection, but an undertaking resembling this is what the Settlement gradually becomes committed to, as its function is revealed through the reaction on its consciousness of its own experiences. Because of this fourfold undertaking, the Settlement has gathered into residence people of widely diversified tastes and interests, and in Hull House, at least, the group has been surprisingly permanent. The majority of the present corp of forty residents support themselves by their business and professional occupations in the city, giving only their leisure time to Settlement undertakings. This in itself tends to continuity of residence and has certain advantages. Among the present staff, of whom the larger number have been in residence for more than twelve years, there are the secretary of the City club, two practicing physicians, several attorneys, newspapermen, businessmen, teachers, scientists, artists, musicians, lecturers in the School of Civics and Philanthropy, officers in the Juvenile Protective Association and in the League for the Protection of Immigrants, a visiting nurse, a sanitary inspector, and others.

===Chinese===

我答说，信条的这种多样是美国定居所处境的一部分，因为我们的任务就是住在多民族多信仰的街区里；在这样驳杂的人群中间，定居所的队伍本身也代表不同的宗教信仰，也许倒是好事。

一位智者告诉我们：「人生来就愿意有一个理性的世界可以去信仰、可以在其中生活」；但要找到一个在理智、审美、道德和实际诸方面都理性的世界，却非易事。倘若所选的地点恰恰是这四方面显然离完善最远的那种地方，这当然更非易事；可是定居所渐渐承担起的，正是这样一种事业，因为它的职能是通过自身经历对意识的反照而显明的。由于这四重事业，定居所把趣味兴味大异的人们聚拢来住；在赫尔馆，这支队伍至少惊人地持久。现有四十名居民的大多数，靠他们在城里的业务和专业为生，只把闲暇时间给定居所的事业。这本身有利于居住的延续，也有某些好处。在现在的队伍里——其中大部分住了不止十二年——有城市俱乐部的书记、两位开业医生、几位律师、报人、商人、教师、科学家、艺术家、音乐家、公民与慈善学院的讲师、少年保护协会和保护移民联盟的官员、一位访视护士、一位卫生稽查员，等等。

===Original===

We have also worked out during our years of residence a plan of living which may be called “cooperative,” for the families and individuals who rent the Hull House apartments have the use of the central kitchen and dining room so far as they care for them; many of them work for hours every week in the studios and shops; the theater and drawing-rooms are available for such social organization as they care to form; the entire group of thirteen buildings is heated and lighted from a central plant. During the years, the common human experiences have gathered about the House; funeral services have been held there, marriages and christenings, and many memories hold us to each other as well as to our neighbors. Each resident, of course, carefully defrays his own expenses, and his relations to his fellow residents are not unlike those of a college professor to his colleagues. The depth and strength of his relation to the neighborhood must depend very largely upon himself and upon the genuine friendships he has been able to make. His relation to the city as a whole comes largely through his identification with those groups who are carrying forward the reforms which a Settlement neighborhood so sadly needs and with which residence has made him familiar.

Life in the Settlement discovers above all what has been called “the extraordinary pliability of human nature,” and it seems impossible to set any bounds to the moral capabilities which might unfold under ideal civic and educational conditions. But in order to obtain these conditions, the Settlement recognizes the need of cooperation, both with the radical and the conservative, and from the very nature of the case the Settlement cannot limit its friends to any one political party or economic school.

===Chinese===

居住的这些年间，我们还琢磨出一种可以称为「合作制」的生活方式：租住赫尔馆各公寓的家庭和个人，随意使用中央厨房和饭厅；他们中许多人每星期在工作室和工场里做工几个钟头；剧场和客厅听凭他们组成他们想组成的社交组织；十三座楼的全部取暖照明由一座中央厂供给。这些年间，共同的人生经历在这座馆周围聚拢；葬礼在这里举行过，婚礼和洗礼也举行过；许多记忆把我们彼此、把我们同邻居拴在一起。每个居民当然仔细自付费用，他同其他居民的关系，同一位大学教授同事间的关系没有两样。他同街区关系的深度和力量，大半要看他自己，看他所能结成的真诚友谊。他同整座城市的关系，大半来自他同那些团体的认同——它们在推进一个定居所街区所迫切需要的种种改革，而居住使他熟悉了这些。

定居所里的生活，首先揭示出所谓的「人性的非凡可塑」；在理想的公民和教育条件下可能展开的道德能力，似乎不可能划出任何界限。但要取得这些条件，定居所认识到必须合作——同激进者合作，也同保守者合作；而且从事物的本性出发，定居所不能把自己的朋友限于任何一个政党或经济学派。

===Original===

The Settlement casts side none of those things which cultivated men have come to consider reasonable and goodly, but it insists that those belong as well to that great body of people who, because of toilsome and underpaid labor, are unable to procure them for themselves. Added to this is a profound conviction that the common stock of intellectual enjoyment should not be difficult of access because of the economic position of him who would approach it, that those “best results of civilization” upon which depend the finer and freer aspects of living must be incorporated into our common life and have free mobility through all elements of society if we would have our democracy endure.

The educational activities of a Settlement, as well its philanthropic, civic, and social undertakings, are but differing manifestations of the attempt to socialize democracy, as is the very existence of the Settlement itself.

===Chinese===

定居所不丢弃教养的人们已认为合理而美好的任何东西，只是它坚持：那些东西同样属于那广大的人群——他们因为劳苦而低酬的劳动，无法为自己弄到它们。此外还有一个深刻的信念：智力享受的共同库存，不应因为接近者的经济地位而难以企及；「文明的最佳成果」——生活中更精微更自由的方面系于它们——必须被编进我们的共同生活，在社会的一切成分里自由流动，倘若我们愿意我们的民主长存。

定居所的教育活动，连同它的慈善、市政和社会事业，都不过是使民主社会化这一企图的不同显现——正如定居所本身的存在一样。

===Original===

To

the memory of

my father

===Chinese===

谨以此书

纪念

我的父亲

## Preface / 序
===Original===

Every preface is, I imagine, written after the book has been completed and now that I have finished this volume I will state several difficulties which may put the reader upon his guard unless he too postpones the preface to the very last.

Many times during the writing of these reminiscences, I have become convinced that the task was undertaken all too soon. One’s fiftieth year is indeed an impressive milestone at which one may well pause to take an accounting, but the people with whom I have so long journeyed have become so intimate a part of my lot that they cannot be written of either in praise or blame; the public movements and causes with which I am still identified have become so endeared, some of them through their very struggles and failures, that it is difficult to discuss them.

It has also been hard to determine what incidents and experiences should be selected for recital, and I have found that I might give an accurate report of each isolated event and yet give a totally misleading impression of the whole, solely by the selection of the incidents. For these reasons and many others I have found it difficult to make a faithful record of the years since the autumn of 1889 when without any preconceived social theories or economic views, I came to live in an industrial district of Chicago.

If the reader should inquire why the book was ever undertaken in the face of so many difficulties, in reply I could instance two purposes, only one of which in the language of organized charity, is “worthy.” Because Settlements have multiplied so easily in the United States I hoped that a simple statement of an earlier effort, including the stress and storm, might be of value in their interpretation and possibly clear them of a certain charge of superficiality. The unworthy motive was a desire to start a “backfire,” as it were, to extinquish two biographies of myself, one of which had been submitted to me in outline, that made life in a Settlement all too smooth and charming.

===Chinese===

我想，每一篇序言都是书写完之后才写的；如今这本册子既已完稿，我便说一说几处难处，好叫读者预先提防——除非他也把序言压到最后一页再读。

写这些追忆的许多时刻，我都确信这桩事动手得太早了。五十岁诚然是一个叫人肃然的里程碑，人尽可以在那里停一停，算一算账；可是同我一路走了这样久的人们，已经成了我命运里太亲的一部分，写他们，不论褒贬，都难下笔；我至今仍在其中的那些公共运动与事业，又已经那样叫我珍爱——有几件，恰恰是通过它们的挣扎与失败——讨论它们也难。

哪些事件与经历该挑出来讲，也一直难以拿定；我发现，每一件孤立的事我都可以报得准确，却可以单单凭着挑选，给整体一个全然误导人的印象。因为这些以及别的许多缘故，我发觉很难忠实记录1889年秋天以来的这些年——那时我没有任何先入为主的社会理论或经济见解，来到芝加哥的一个工业区住了下来。

倘若读者问，既有这么多难处，这书到底为什么要写，我可以举出两个目的，用组织化慈善的行话说，只有一个是「正当的」。因为定居所在美国繁殖得这样容易，我希望把一次早期努力——连同它的紧张与风暴——简单地陈述出来，也许有助于解释它们，或许还能洗掉加在它们身上的一层肤浅之嫌。那个不正当的动机，是想放一把「迎面火」，好灭掉写我的两部传记——其中一部已把提纲送来给我看——它们把定居所里的生活写得实在太顺太迷人。

===Original===

The earlier chapters present influences and personal motives with a detail which will be quite unpardonable if they fail to make clear the personality upon whom various social and industrial movements in Chicago reacted during a period of twenty years. No effort is made in the recital to separate my own history from that of Hull House during the years in which I was “launched deep into the stormy intercourse of human life” for, so far as a mind is pliant under the pressure of events and experiences, it becomes hard to detach it.

It has unfortunately been necessary to abandon the chronological order in favor of the topical, for during the early years at Hull House, time seemed to afford a mere framework for certain lines of activity and I have found in writing this book, that after these activities have been recorded, I can scarcely recall the scaffolding.

More than a third of the material in the book has appeared in *The American Magazine*, one chapter of it in *McClure’s Magazine*, and earlier statements of the Settlement motive, published years ago, have been utilized in chronological order because it seemed impossible to reproduce their enthusiasm.

It is a matter of gratification to me that the book is illustrated from drawings made by Miss Norah Hamilton of Hull House, and the cover designed by another resident, Mr. Frank Hazenplug.1 I am indebted for the making of the index and for many other services to Miss Clara Landsberg, also of Hull House.

If the conclusions of the whole matter are similar to those I have already published at intervals during the twenty years at Hull House, I can only make the defense that each of the earlier books was an attempt to set forth a thesis supported by experience, whereas this volume endeavors to trace the experiences through which various conclusions were forced upon me.

===Chinese===

前面几章铺陈影响与个人的动机，铺陈得那样细；倘若它们没能画清楚这个人格——二十年里芝加哥种种社会运动与工业运动就是在这样一个人身上起反应的——那这份细就全然不可原谅了。叙述中我没有设法把我自己的经历同赫尔会所的经历分开，在那那些年里，我「被深深地掷进了人类生活风雨的交往中」；因为一颗心既经不起事变与经历的压，它就难得再剥得下来。

可惜不得不舍弃编年的次序，改用专题的次序：赫尔会所早年，时间仿佛只是几条活动线索的架子；写这本书时我又发现，这些活动既经记录下来，那副脚手架我几乎记不起了。

书中材料有三分之一强曾刊于《美国杂志》（*The American Magazine*），其中一章刊于《麦克卢尔杂志》（*McClure's Magazine*）；多年前发表过的几篇对定居所动机的早期陈述，也按先后用了进来，因为那份热忱，如今再也复现不出。

使我高兴的是，本书的插图出自赫尔会所的诺拉·汉密尔顿小姐（Miss Norah Hamilton）的手绘，封面由另一位居民弗兰克·黑曾普卢格先生（Mr. Frank Hazenplug）设计。1 索引的编制和许多别的帮助，我要感谢赫尔会所的克拉拉·兰茨贝格小姐（Miss Clara Landsberg）。

倘若全书的结论同我二十年间在赫尔会所陆续发表的相似，我唯一能辩解的是：早先的每一本书，都是想摆出一个以经历为凭的论题；而这一本，是想追溯那些把种种结论强加给我的经历。
